# Build phases in detail

Every phase with what it delivers, its version and its state; each has its own notes page. The short list is on the
[README](../README.md#releases). What EA Explorer does today, area by area: [capabilities.md](capabilities.md).


Each phase has its own notes: what it delivers, a demo, checks, how to upgrade and what is not in it.

Pictures open in the platform's draw.io in a new tab instead of downloading a file (D256). ## Planned phases (from the build plan)
| Phase | Name |
| --- | --- |
| 15.3 (rest) | ~~Business architecture, the rest~~ — replaced (D358, kept simple by D415, 10 Nov 2026) by a project tab **Other views and documents**: sample document templates (Word, PowerPoint and similar, our own wording, no branding) to download and use; it does nothing in the project or the metamodel. **Built 10 Nov 2026 (batch 1, D421), release 0.17.0, accepted and closed (owner, 10 Nov 2026)** (D429): ten sample templates made as they are downloaded. The project's **own uploads** beside the templates (D461, 0.17.0): anyone on the project uploads and downloads; the uploader, Project Admin, Lead Architect or a Super Admin replaces (versions kept) or deletes [phase-15.3.md](phase-15.3.md) |
| 19, 20 | Merge a child into its parent, as a wizard (design agreed 9 Nov 2026, D359): the child's owner sends it for merge; three-way review against the child's starting point (one-sided changes pre-accepted, "both changed" a conflict to decide); the parent's Model Admins or Committers accept or reject each change, new entity types and attributes included (metamodel additions need a Model Admin); a merge report (accepted, rejected with reasons) kept as a document; then the wizard asks: freeze the child, or refresh it from the parent and carry on. Dropped: copies by scope, overlap warnings, pulling down without merging, partial merges. **Built 10 Nov 2026 (batch 1, D421), release 0.17.0, accepted and closed (owner, 10 Nov 2026)** (D422 to D425): [phase-19-20.md](phase-19-20.md). |
| 21 | Dedupe within a model (design agreed 9 Nov 2026, D361): a wizard finds candidates by name (near spellings too) and attributes; pick a pair or group, choose the survivor and each field's value; relationships move to it and duplicate relationships go; the others are removed; old display IDs still find the survivor; it is the person's change, committed like any other and rolled back from Commits; anyone who may change the model; data entry warns when a new name looks like an existing one. **Built 10 Nov 2026 (batch 1, D421), release 0.17.0, accepted and closed (owner, 10 Nov 2026)** (D426 to D428): [phase-21.md](phase-21.md). |
| 22 | ~~Ownership and collaboration~~ — closed 9 Nov 2026 as covered (D362): models and data belong to the organisation, not people; the change log, commits with who and a message, entity history and document check-in and check-out history are the record |
| 23 | ~~Portfolio, lifecycle and impact~~ — closed 9 Nov 2026 as covered (D363): fields, TIME, lifecycle and end of life, cost, obsolescence, single points of failure, rationalisation, impact pictures and dependency reports are built; relationship dates, calculated attributes, impact grading and cloning dropped. A scenario planning tool is to be discussed instead |
| 24 | KPI trends, an OData feed and the report designer (design agreed 9 Nov 2026, D365): a standard KPI set per model (counts by type, applications by TIME and lifecycle, total cost, quality score, orphans, end-of-life technology) kept on the 1st of each month and on every tag, plus Model Admins' own KPIs (a count or sum over a type with a filter), as trends on the model page and in Superset; an OData feed so Power BI and Excel read entity types as tables, signed in, read only, with the person's permissions; a report designer screen (a type, its attributes, related types, filters) that builds a Superset dataset and opens Superset's chart builder. Scheduled reports dropped **Built 10 Nov 2026 (D430 to D436): KPIs tab, report KR-01, the OData feed with Basic sign-in, the report designer opening Superset's chart builder**; version 0.17.0; **accepted and closed (owner, 10 Nov 2026)**; [phase-24.md](phase-24.md) |
| 25 | Machine access (design agreed 9 Nov 2026, D366; replaces "security hardening"): service accounts (made by Super Admins and IT Admins) and personal credentials, as **tokens, API keys or client certificates** (mutual TLS at the proxy), each with an expiry, revoke, last used and every call in the change log. An admin screen gives each credential **read or read-write per API area** (models and content, metamodel, projects, documents, reports, library, change log, administration, MCP), optionally limited to some models or projects, listing the endpoints in each area; never more than its owner's roles allow. Entra ID or Google sign-in, SCIM, MFA, hidden types and fields, rate limits dropped Client certificates may be **self-signed** (each one uploaded and trusted individually on the admin screen) (D378). **Built 10 Nov 2026 (D438 to D441): System admin › Machine access (tokens, API keys, client certificates on the proxy's own port 9443, service accounts, nine areas read or read-write, limits to models or projects, every call in the change log), the OData feed's Basic sign-in and MCP take them**; version 0.17.0; **accepted and closed (owner, 10 Nov 2026)**; [phase-25.md](phase-25.md) |
| 27.1 | Scenario planning with AI (design agreed 9 Nov 2026, D364): in a project, through MCP, with a guided prompt *plan scenarios*. The assistant reads and compares the project's scenario models against the baseline (what each adds, changes and retires; totals of cost, applications, capabilities covered, risks; impact); makes scenario models (a child or copy of the baseline, after the person says yes); applies what-ifs ("retire X, replace with Y") as the person's uncommitted changes; and records the assessment (comparison, scores, recommendation) as a project document, optionally a governance log decision for approval. Anything else it needs, as the person, within their permissions **Accepted and closed (owner, 10 Nov 2026; 0.18.0, D490 to D495, migration 0041, [phase-27.1.md](phase-27.1.md))**: the prompt plan_scenarios and seven tools (preview then confirm), figures and comparison with the person's own what-ifs, scenario models tagged-first, assessments with a Word document and a governance decision, and a Scenarios tab in the project |
| 26, 27 | MCP (design agreed 9 Nov 2026, D368; the server, its 82 tools (79 then; 3 drawn view tools in 0.16.0) and 3 prompts are built): an **OAuth remote connector** (server installs: add EA Explorer as a custom connector in an AI app and sign in through Keycloak in the browser, with discovery metadata), an **AI activity log** (every MCP call per person: tool, a summary of the question, result size, changes made; seen by the person and admins) and **citations** (answers carry links back to entities, views and documents). Usage limits and a separate staging queue dropped (an assistant's changes are the person's uncommitted changes) **Model operations through MCP** (D458): copy, dedupe, merge into the parent with every change accepted (copy overrides the parent), refresh, tag, freeze, compare and the other wizards from plain requests; preview then confirm, tagged first, the person's own rights Further capabilities proposed in [mcp-capabilities.md](mcp-capabilities.md) (D459). **As built (10 Nov 2026, D482 to D489): about 120 tools and 11 prompts: model operations (copy, dedupe, override the parent, refresh, merge requests, every model wizard, common analysis), clean-up, analysis (impact, rationalisation, cost, technology risk, single points of failure, heat maps, what changed, KPI trends), Word reports into a project, governance help, surveys and maturity, administration questions; preview then confirm, tagged first, the person's own rights, read-only credentials read only; the AI activity log (MCP page > AI activity); citations; the OAuth remote connector (discovery, Keycloak client eax-mcp)**; version 0.18.0; **accepted and closed (owner, 10 Nov 2026)**; [phase-26-27.md](phase-26-27.md) |
| 28, 29 | Drawn views in draw.io, parts 1 and 2 ([phase-28.md](phase-28.md), D288, D354, D355): design agreed 9 Nov 2026 — views drawn with the standard ArchiMate notation from the model's own entities, relationships added as uncommitted changes, "view" everywhere. Built already: the diagrams drawn from the repository (network, domains, impact, maps, matrices, value streams, roadmaps, views), each opening in draw.io. **Back end built in 0.16.0** (migration 0033, D401 to D404): entity type looks, drawn views with versions and save conflicts, resync, allowed relationships, junctions, entity icons, heat maps, views as project evidence, tags, copies and merges, three MCP tools, sample views; the draw.io plugin built; **the screens built** (10 Nov 2026: Views tab list, the editor with search, properties, save conflicts, resync, heat map, export, versions, evidence; save as a drawn view; entity icons; type looks). **Views service** (10 Nov 2026, D405 to D409): drawn views and the models' own views moved to services/views (database eaxviews, `/api/views/v1`); every picture saves as a drawn view (tidied, ArchiMate notation) or downloads, "Open in draw.io" gone; evidence a link only; the stencil's groups shown or hidden per person **Accepted and closed (owner, 10 Nov 2026)**, with the full-screen tab, two-way sync, layouts, relationship tabs, name : type labels, undo and the similar-name check (D411 to D414, D420, D454, D460) |
| 30, 31 | ~~Standard views, parts 1 and 2~~ — closed 9 Nov 2026 as covered (D357): the 49 generated views (8.2), comparison and gaps (8.3) and drawn views with copying (28) do what they were for; bulk generation, decks, view templates and cross-model views dropped |
| 33, 33.1 | ~~ArchiMate exchange and conformance~~ — **dropped from the plan until The Open Group publishes the ArchiMate 4 exchange specification** (owner, 9 Nov 2026, D385, D386); the design (D369) is kept for then |
| 34, 34.1 | Published import formats, not an ETL tool (design agreed 9 Nov 2026, D370): we publish the formats and other systems write to them: the **ArchiMate 4 exchange format** for models (only once it is published, D385), **Excel** for projects and models (today's sheets), and a new **CSV format** (a master file listing per-type entity and relationship files), kept **as close as possible to today's Excel imports**: the same columns, drop-downs' values, provisional IDs, mappings, preview and undo, one CSV per sheet. Delivered by dropping files at an agreed place and calling an API to trigger the load, by uploading in the screens, or by calling our API with a service account (25). A delivery is checked and matched by **type and name**, shown as a load waiting for review (new, changed, errors), and accepted by a person with the right as their committed load (undo as today); a delivery only adds and changes (deleting is done in the platform). A source can be set so **its values win** for the fields it delivers. Connectors, when built, write these formats. Apache Hop (D128), field ownership and source keys dropped Deliveries always arrive in **a folder** (which IT may map or mount to SFTP or a share); the CSV format is **the Excel format as separate files**, one CSV per sheet, plus a **master file** saying which CSV holds what (D378). **Create a model from a metamodel workbook** (D419): our template or a model's exported metamodel, blank or added to an existing model, metamodel only, preview first, whoever can create a model; version 0.18.0 (migration 0042_import_formats, D462 to D468); **accepted and closed (owner, 10 Nov 2026)**: import sources and deliveries (folder, API, upload; `eax import`), matched by type and name, kept values, accepted as a committed load or declined; the CSV format's master.csv; empty templates on the fly; the ArchiMate exchange format refused until published; a model from a metamodel workbook; [phase-34.md](phase-34.md), [import-formats.md](import-formats.md) |
| 35, 35.1 | Operations insight and housekeeping (design agreed 9 Nov 2026, D371): **usage insight** for IT and Super Admins (who uses what and how often, features, model use and size, models unused for 180 days listed as a report only, stale uncommitted work and old loads), **Prometheus metrics** (/metrics for IT monitoring), **retention rules** for everything that grows (keep, archive or delete after N days) with safe defaults that only archive (loads and task files after 90 days, notifications after a year, old history versions after 3 years) and delete nothing until an admin sets it, a scheduled **housekeeping job** whose first run is a dry run to confirm, an archive searchable on request, and a **database health page** (largest tables, slowest queries, last tidied) **Built 10 Nov 2026 (D442 to D445): System admin › Operations (usage, housekeeping with a confirmed dry run and a nightly job, the archive, database health), /api/metrics; old history versions removable after an age a Super Admin sets in years, pinned versions always kept (D445)**; version 0.17.0; **accepted and closed (owner, 10 Nov 2026)**; [phase-35.md](phase-35.md) |
| 36 | Deployment kinds and updates (D372 as amended by D416 and D417, 10 Nov 2026): **laptop**, **server** (one or more hosts, Docker Swarm or similar), **AWS** and **Azure**, each guided and from the command line; **updates** from the web console or `eax update`: the git URL, channels Prod / RC / Latest (RC and Latest enabled by an admin), built on the host, forward only, full backup first, one release at a time, stop and ask on failure, roll back to the last working version ([phase-36-updates.md](phase-36-updates.md)); moving an installation; passwords from a key vault; Active Directory groups mapped to roles **DigitalOcean** added as a fifth kind (D457): Droplets with our Docker stack, cloud firewall, volumes, Spaces for backups, load balancer; guided and command line **`eax deploy`** (D498): a deployment wizard, web and console, for laptop, server (local or over SSH, installs Docker if needed), and AWS, DigitalOcean or Azure (creates the machines or uses an existing one) **Updates built (0.19.0, D510 to D516, migration 0044)**: System admin › Updates and `eax update` / `eax rollback` (Prod, RC, Latest from the git URL; forward only; a pinned full backup with the code first; one release at a time in an updater container; Retry or Roll back; now or at a time; banner and What's new), [phase-36-updates.md](phase-36-updates.md) As built; to be accepted **Deployment built (10 Nov 2026, the last set, D500 to D506; version 0.19.0, migration 0043): `eax deploy` (console or web page, five kinds, --plan, Retry/Back/Quit), the installer eax-deploy (no repository needed), moving an installation, passwords from a key vault (`eax secrets`), directory groups mapped to roles; to be accepted**; [phase-36.md](phase-36.md), [deployment.md](deployment.md) **Deploy from the application** (D555 to D557): System admin › Deploy for Super and IT Admins, to a server over SSH or AWS, Azure, DigitalOcean, through the operations helper and a deployer container; plan, progress, Retry/Back/Quit, log, credentials for the run only, history of targets; fleets R2 **Command centre** (D554, 0.19.0): every eax command from System admin with a guided form, a Console (eax commands as typed and `eax console`'s menu, never a shell) and the history; delete-everything commands for Super Admins behind a strong confirm and a backup first; the same tabs on `eax deploy`'s page **Banner, schedules and settings that survive resets** (D558 to D562): System admin > Banner; Command centre > Schedules and `eax schedule` (a cron in the platform; the demo reset every night); persist/ beside work/ (schedules.json, banner.json, safety-backups, test-results) in every infrastructure backup; | R2.9 | **Built 13 Nov 2026 (D629)**: a type derived from another in the metamodel (Change type › Derived from), its attributes and allowed relationships flowing down, retired ones kept, the Specialization line on the ER diagram, sample Server > Windows Server > Database Server. **Release 2 feature: an entity derived from another entity** (owner, 11 Nov 2026: "an entity in model is derived from another entity for example Server -> Windos Server -> DB Server or Web Server"). In a model, an entity can be made **from another entity of the same type** (its base), at any depth: *Server* → *Windows Server* → *DB Server* or *Web Server*. The derived entity takes the base's attributes (and, to discuss, its relationships), keeps what it sets itself, and shows where each value comes from; a change to the base flows down to what derives from it unless overridden. Shown as a tree (base and derived entities), in the entity's page and in drawn views (ArchiMate's Specialization relationship between the two). **To discuss with the owner before building, one question at a time:** whether derivation is its own link or the ArchiMate Specialization relationship; what is inherited (attributes only, or relationships too); whether a base may be in another model (a library or reference model) and what a change there does; whether derived entities are templates (a "Windows Server" standard) or real things (each server), or both; how it shows in grids, imports and exports, compare and merge, quality and reports. Not built until the owner says **Owner's answers (12 Nov 2026, 21:37):** derivation is a **design-time decision between entity types in the metamodel**, not between entities and not when values are captured ("The inheritance is a design time decision not at capturing values"): (1) recorded as ArchiMate's **Specialization** between the two types, so it shows in the metamodel diagram and the exchange format; (2) the derived type gets the base type's **attributes and allowed relationships**, plus its own; (3) base and derived type are in the **same metamodel**; (4) any type may be a base; (5) when data is entered, the form shows the **superset** of attributes (the base type's and its own); **each entity stores all its own values**, nothing is stored in or read from a base, so deleting or changing a base never touches an entity's values; (6) a change to the base type (an attribute added or removed) **flows down to the derived types when the metamodel version is published**; a removed attribute keeps the values already captured, shown as retired, so nothing is lost; (7) in grids, views and reports **each type stands alone**: a report on the base type does not include the derived type's entities. Ready to build after the bugs |

The command line (eax.ps1/eax.cmd, eax.sh, eaxcli, eaxpy, D331) shows **`eax status` in colour**: each service and start-up step green when up or done, amber while starting or waiting, red when down or failed, grey when switched off; the same colours as the web's start-up chip and Health page. **Every other eax output in colour too**: headings, OK / warning / error lines, prompts and choices in the guided commands (deploy, update, packs, test, backup, restore), the help text's command names and examples, progress, and the final result line. Colour only on a terminal that supports it (Windows Terminal, PowerShell 7, modern conhost, Linux and macOS terminals); plain text when the output is piped or redirected, in CI, or with `--no-color` / `NO_COLOR` set, so logs and scripts are unchanged. Kept in step in all four forms, documented in eaxcli/help.txt and docs/command-line-and-console.md, with tests that the plain output is unchanged. Not built until the owner says |

(1) **A test-data flag** on every project and model (and what hangs off them: entities, relationships, views, documents, uploads, surveys, users and credentials the run made), set when the test harness creates it and never by hand; shown as a *Test data* chip, and left out of lists, search, reports, KPIs, the OData feed and the MCP tools unless asked for. (2) **The harness cleans up after itself**, whether the run passes, fails or is stopped: at the end of each run, and at the start of the next for anything an earlier run left. (3) **Physical deletion**: test data is removed from the repository for good (database rows, files in Documents and the library, Superset, backups' test-only items), not archived or soft-deleted, an exception to "never take away" (D289) that applies only to flagged test data. A watcher (in the operations helper, so it keeps working when the API is down) checks every container every minute: stopped or exited, restarting again and again, unhealthy (its health check failing), not answering its own heartbeat, out of memory, or (with R2.16) its host short of CPU, memory or disk. One alert per problem, not one a minute: repeated only after a set time while it lasts, and a second "working again" notice when it recovers. Optionally restart the container on its own (a setting per container, off by default, at most N times an hour, every restart recorded). The rules (which containers, how long before alerting, who is told, the webhook) in System settings; every alert in the audit trail and in the history on Health. | R2.20 | **Fixed and accepted (owner, 13 Nov 2026, 15:38: "R2.4 2.23 and 2.20 are fixed and accepted").** **Release 2 bug: the reports-init container always shows a problem** (owner, 11 Nov 2026: "fix why reports-init container is always in problem"). reports-init is a **one-shot** container: it sets up Superset (the reporting tool) and then exits, and the reports container starts only after it has finished successfully (docker-compose.yml: `service_completed_successfully`). Parts of EA Explorer already treat it as one-shot (the start-up status, the console's checks in services/ops), but somewhere it is still shown as a problem. Find out which on the owner's laptop: (a) it **finished well (exit code 0)** but a screen (Containers, Health, the startup chip, `eax status`, the self-test) counts any stopped container as a problem: then show it as **Done** (green, "set-up finished at …") everywhere, in one shared rule for one-shot containers (reports-init and setup), with a test; or (b) it **really fails** (exit code not 0: superset-init.sh, its database or Superset's own upgrade): then fix the cause, show its last log lines and the reason, offer Run again, and make the reports container's state say it is waiting for it. Covered by the alerts (R2.19, which must not alert for a finished one-shot container) and the self-test. **12 Nov 2026:** found: the Superset image's own health check ran in the one-shot container, so it showed "unhealthy" after finishing with exit code 0; switched off (D595) |
| R2.21 | **Fixed 12 Nov 2026**: CPU shown as a share of the whole machine with the cores in use (services/ops/ops.py cpu_of_host, ContainerConsole.jsx; tests/test_cpu_share.py with a fake 8-core host); the scaling rules keep reading a container's own CPU (a share of one core), as their thresholds were set. **Release 2 bug: total CPU shows more than 100%** (owner, 11 Nov 2026: "Total CPU > 100%"). System Admin › Running › Containers adds up each container's CPU % into a Total (services/web/src/ContainerConsole.jsx, `sum("cpu_percent")`). Docker reports a container's CPU as a share of **one** core (`docker stats` CPUPerc: 250% means two and a half cores busy), so the total runs past 100% on any machine with more than one core and reads as a fault. Fix: show CPU as a share of the **whole host** (Docker's figure divided by the host's number of CPUs, from `docker info` NCPU or the container's online CPUs), so each container and the total stay between 0% and 100%, with the cores in use beside it ("1.2 of 8 cores"); the same on the container's own page, Hosts (R2.16), the scaling rules (services/ops/scaling.py, which read CPUPerc: check their thresholds still mean what they say) and `eax status`; the column's help says what the figure means. Tests with a fake 8-core host |

| R2.41 | **Include archived built 13 Nov 2026 (D647): in Find and on the model and project lists.** **Built 13 Nov 2026: models (D626) and projects (D628)** move to the archive database and back; a project takes its documents and the models only it uses (made by it, used by no other project, nothing derived from them); the rest stay and are listed first. Still open: Include archived in Find and search. **Release 2: four states for models and projects, and archived and deleted ones in a database of their own** (owner, 12 Nov 2026: "archived or deleted models and projects move to another physical DB so that they dont slow things down. When archive content is to be seen they are fetched from there ... When something is archived or deleted etc, the work happens in background. Archive is not freeze"). The states: **Active** (worked on), **Frozen** (kept where it is and readable, no more edits or updates), **Archived** (moved out of the working database into an **archive database**, a separate PostgreSQL database that can be on its own server; listed and opened read only, its content fetched from there when someone looks; can be brought back to Active), **Deleted** (moved to the archive database too, hidden from everyone but Super Admins, removed for good after a retention period, or restored). Archiving, deleting and restoring run as **background tasks** with progress (D273), with the audit trail and a notification when done; what refers to the moved model or project (merge requests, project links, views, library entries) is handled and listed before it moves. Today's flags (is_deleted, archived, frozen, project status) are mapped to the four states; backups and restores include the archive database; the reports and OData leave out archived and deleted content unless asked. Measure the speed before and after. With R2.27 (the operations database). **Owner's answers (12 Nov 2026, 21:20):** (1) **who**: a model's or project's own admins (Model Admin, Project Admin) freeze, unfreeze, archive and bring back from the archive; only a Super Admin deletes, restores a deleted one, or purges; (2) **retention**: a deleted model or project is **kept in the archive database until a Super Admin purges it** by hand (no automatic removal; the owner first chose 90 days, then: "sorry keep until purged"); (3) **what moves with a project**: the project, its documents, and the models only it uses; a model another project also uses stays where it is, and the list of those is shown before the move; (4) **where**: the archive database is a database of its own in the same PostgreSQL by default, with **its own connection string** (EAX_ARCHIVE_DB_URL) so it can move to another server or container by changing the setting (R2.50); (5) **search**: archived ones are left out of Find, search and the model and project lists unless **Include archived** is ticked, which fetches them from the archive database. Ready to build after the bugs |

Also: publishing a model to the library copies it into the library database; lookups that read both (the library pages, Find with library models, compare with a library model) read the library database; backups and restores include it; the move of existing library models is a migration done in the background at start-up with progress. | R2.44 | **Built 13 Nov 2026 (D635): the file store (EAX_FILE_STORE), settings from Docker secrets, the always-on test runner service with EAX_PLATFORM=managed, backups only in a mounted folder (EAX_BACKUP_DIR, asked by eax init).** **Release 2, to think through: EA Explorer on Docker Swarm (or another orchestrator) with no local file system** (owner, 12 Nov 2026: "think how everything will work like tests when they are deployed on a docker swarm or something and we have no local file system"). Today much relies on folders of the installation on one host: work/ (the config file, logs, test reports and videos, laptop data), persist/ (settings that survive resets), backups/, the repository itself (mounted into the containers: docs, packs, testbench/data), and the operations helper, which runs `docker` on that host (starting test runners, Terminal, logs, updates, scaling). List each of these and decide where it lives instead: object storage (S3-compatible, MinIO on a laptop), the databases (R2.27, R2.28: settings, schedules, test results), images that carry what they need (docs, packs, test data baked in), Docker secrets and configs, and the orchestrator's own API instead of the Docker socket (a test run as a one-off service or job, logs from the orchestrator, R2.32). The same code must still run on one laptop. Write the findings as a design for the owner (with R2.1 farms, R2.2 remote instances, R2.3 the test bench, R2.22 smaller containers) before any story **Owner's answers (12 Nov 2026, 21:28):** (1) **files**: "users should be able to work on local file system, but purely serverless docker environment uses a separate DB": one setting chooses the **file store**: the local work folder (the default, laptop and server, as today) or a **files database** of its own (its own connection string, R2.50) for a Docker environment with no local folder; test reports, screenshots and videos, logs, backups, imports and exports go through it, so nothing else changes between the two; (2) **settings**: in such an environment passwords come from **Docker secrets** and the other settings from a **Docker config** or the environment, with the same names as eaexplorer.env; the laptop and server keep the file. (3) **no Docker socket** (owner, 21:46): the test runner runs as an **always-on service** that waits for runs; the Command centre and Containers pages say the platform manages the containers and show the commands to use; (4) **backups** (owner, 21:47: "On the selected folder server that is mounted, for laptop on file system. On server or docker ask for a file system that is mounted as a part of initial config"): backups never go into a database: on a laptop they go to the local folder as today; on a server or a Docker platform the **first-time setup asks for a mounted folder** (a network share or a volume) for backups, and EA Explorer will not take backups until one is given |

GNU tar says this when it could not read some files of a set (a file it may not read, a socket, or a file removed while it read); services/backup/backup.sh tars documents, tls, reports (Superset's home), imports, exports and persist/, and the message hides which set and which file. To do: show the tar lines before the message (the backup's log, with the set's name) so the cause is visible; find the cause on the laptop (likely a set written to while it is read, such as Superset's home, or files the backup container cannot read); leave out what need not be backed up (caches, sockets, logs), treat "file changed as we read it" as a warning, not a failure; add a test that fails without the fix (D599) and the self-test backup check (R2.45). **Owner: "If we can not do reliable backups and restores from web, then we should just show the command to run. This can be an issue in real life."** So: unless a backup and a restore from the web pass the self-test's backup checks (R2.45) on the laptop and a server, the Backups page offers no Back up now or Restore… buttons and instead shows the command to run (`.\eax backup [tag]`, `.\eax restore <name|tag|latest> --what …`) with a copy button; the buttons come back only once the web path is proven reliable. **12 Nov 2026, first part done**: each file set is archived by services/backup/tarset.sh, so a failure now names the set and the files tar could not read ("files reports (/superset-home) could not be read: ./x: Cannot open: Permission denied") instead of "error exit delayed", and a file that changed while it was read is a warning, not a failure (tests/test_backup_tarset.py). The web-or-command rule **built (D608)**: the Backups page shows the commands until the self-test's backup checks pass. Still to do: the cause on the owner's laptop (from that message) |

Import and export › **Discovery**: anyone who may change the model sets a source up (the sign-in is the name of a setting in the config file, never the secret), **Look** discovers its sets, fields, keys and the fields that hold other sets' keys and suggests the mapping (set to entity type, field to attribute, key field to relationship type), a person changes and saves it; **Run now**, or daily or weekly (every 10 minutes the job runner runs those due, as the person who set them up), makes a **delivery** of what is new or changed since the last (core.discovery_seen; *Run in full* sends all), previewed and accepted or declined as every delivery (D465); a found name close to an element of its type is **suggested as a match with its confidence** (the duplicate finder's closeness, D177) and a person says same or new (core.discovery_matches); renames at the source are listed. The **AI engine** is the Super Admins' choice (off, cloud with the organisation's own key named by a setting, or local; the common chat-completions interface). Migration 0071. Run end to end on a real database (an OData source delivered, previewed, accepted; unchanged proposed nothing; a change proposed one; a near name suggested at 96% and confirmed; a push; an AI source with a fake engine). Tests in tests/test_discovery.py and the self-test's black-box `discovery:` checks in the run's test model. **Release 3 epic: Data Discovery and Ingestion** (owner, 12 Nov 2026: "Make release 3 Epic as Data Discover and Ingestion. We will have to figure it out"). To be worked out with the owner before any story is written: finding what the organisation already has (its applications, data, technology, people and processes, wherever they are recorded) and bringing it into the models, kept up to date. Starting points to discuss: the import sources and deliveries that exist today (phase 25, D583), the industry packs and the metamodel workbook, the MCP server, and which outside systems to read from and how often; what is discovered automatically and what a person confirms; how duplicates and changes are handled (with quality and duplicates, D177); and what stays out of scope. Nothing is planned in detail yet **Owner (12 Nov 2026, 21:40):** all four areas are in scope: applications, technology and infrastructure, data, and business (processes, organisation); the rest is to be discussed later ("lets skip R3 discussion for now") **Decided (owner, 13 Nov 2026, 21:22 to 21:31)**: (1) sources, all of them: files people drop (Excel, CSV, ArchiMate exchange files, building on today's import sources and deliveries), cloud and infrastructure inventories, configuration databases and service desks, and an **AI scan of folders, APIs and document stores of unstructured documents** pointed at EA Explorer, where the platform discovers content and adds it to a model of interest; (2) **each scan belongs to an EA model**: its sources are set up for that model by **everyone with write access to the model**; (3) whatever is found arrives as **proposals a person accepts**, edits or rejects, then commits (as imports work today), each with its source; (4) a source runs **on demand and on a schedule** of its own (daily, weekly), each run finding only what changed since the last; (5) a finding that may already be in the model is **suggested as a match** to the existing element, with its confidence, using the duplicate finder (D177), and the person confirms it or says it is new; (6) the **AI engine is the admin's choice** per installation: a cloud AI with the organisation's own key, or one run locally; off until an admin turns it on. **Order of Release 3**: the testing stories first (R3.8 to R3.11, R3.13 to R3.16), then the epic, and the security testing of the whole release (R3.12) last. **The owner, 13 Nov 2026, 22:21: "what I want for Data ingestion is a reverse OData feed: it discovers and then writes to a model"**: the counterpart of the OData feed EA Explorer already publishes (phase 24, D365): where today's feed lets other tools read a model's content in OData, ingestion is the same in reverse: EA Explorer reads OData feeds (and the other sources) that are pointed at it, discovers the entities, attributes and relationships in them, maps them to the target model's metamodel (the entity types and attributes, with the mapping kept per source and suggested for a person to confirm), and writes what it found to that model as proposals; on the next run only what changed is proposed. A source's feed can also be pushed to EA Explorer (an OData-style endpoint per model that accepts entities and relationships), with the same proposals and confirmation |

| R3.7 | **Accepted (owner, 14 Nov 2026, 08:14: "All other items are accepted")**. **Built 13 Nov 2026 (D665)**: migration 0070 adds, as indexes only: the hashed name (md5 of lower(name), kept as a 16-byte uuid) per model for exact name matches, the hashed attribute value per attribute type, lower(display_id) per model, and trigram indexes on display_id, description and the merged-away names and ids, so every column a search ORs with the name has one. Finding an entity by name (repository, scenarios) compares the key and then the name. On a 200,000-row copy both kinds of query use the indexes together (BitmapOr). The hash is an index on the expression rather than a column the application fills (see D665). The self-test's database checks fail if an index is missing. Tests in tests/test_text_keys.py. **Moved to Release 3 from R2.52 (owner, 13 Nov 2026, 15:38: "Move 2.58, 2.22 and 2.52 To R3").** **Measuring built 13 Nov 2026 (D627)**: `eax databases speed`; the choices per kind to discuss with the owner. **Release 2, to discuss then build: faster text comparisons in the database** (owner, 12 Nov 2026, 21:33: "db indexing speed, if we do where on strings then those need to be 1. converted into str_md5 2. indexed on this new col 3. Used in search"; 21:34, after the options: "update the story to discuss string comparison we will discuss and implement"). First list every query that compares text (exact matches and joins on names, attribute values, keys and paths; imports and dedupe matching by name; case-insensitive matches; contains and near spellings in Find and search; words in long text), with how often each runs and how long it takes on the laptop's data. Then choose with the owner, per kind of comparison: **exact match**: a hash index on the column itself (any length, no extra column) or a B-tree index (also sorting and starts-with; values under about 2,700 bytes); **case-insensitive**: an index on lower(column); **contains and near spellings**: a trigram index (pg_trgm, GIN); **words in long text**: a full-text index (tsvector, GIN); **very long values matched exactly**: a hash column filled by the application (the owner's first idea, MD5; or PostgreSQL's 64-bit hashtextextended, or SHA-256), indexed, the query comparing the hash and then the text. An index holds no logic, so all of these keep D248. Measure before and after; with R2.24 part 4 (foreign keys without an index). In discussion **Decided (owner, 13 Nov 2026, 21:22 to 21:31)**: both: exact matches (names, keys) through a hashed column with its index, as the owner described, and "contains" searches (Find, filters) through a trigram index. |
| R3.8 | **Accepted (owner, 14 Nov 2026, 08:14: "All other items are accepted")**. **Built 13 Nov 2026 (D659)**: the suite "restore", the last of a run, the command line only (`eax test restore`; `eax test full` from the command line ends with it, asked first, --yes skips the question): the run's models and project fingerprinted (counts and a checksum of guids, names and values), an infrastructure backup (also the safety backup) and an application backup, then for each restore the content changed first and compared after: restore all, one model over itself, and the infrastructure backup part by part (db, app+data, files, full), signing in checked after each; results kept in memory and recorded after the last restore (an infrastructure restore puts back the run's own record as it was); the safety backup put back whole when a step fails; the backups it made removed. From the web it is not offered, and refused if asked. Migration 0068 (the suite in the constraints). Tests in tests/test_restore_check.py; the fingerprint and the changes checked on PostgreSQL. **Release 3: a complete, automated backup and restore test, from the command line only** (owner, 13 Nov 2026, 15:51: "write a new story to test backup and restore properly and completly in an automated manner from command line ... As this can not be done from web we will add this to command line only"). A new `eax test backup-restore` that runs from start to end with nobody at the keyboard, on a laptop or a server: (1) **start**: bring EA Explorer up (or use the one running) and wait until the services it checks are ready; (2) **make content through the APIs**: models with entities, relationships, attributes, tags and history; a project with steps, people, sign-off and documents in Documents (several versions of a file); reports, views, settings, schedules, a personal setting, a user role on a model and on a project; (3) **take a fingerprint** of everything made (counts and checksums per model, project, document file and version, each database schema, the files and the config file); (4) **back up**: an infrastructure backup and an application backup, each checked when done; (5) **change the installation**: add new models and documents, edit and delete some of what was made, change settings; (6) **restore**: the infrastructure backup (whole, then each part: app, app+data, db, files), and the application backup (all, one model as new, one model over itself, one project); (7) **check all the content**: after each restore, compare with the fingerprint; what was made before the backup is back exactly, what came after is gone (or kept, for a restore as new), the changes are undone, every document file and version opens, the services are healthy and people can sign in; (8) **report**: each step with what it checks, passed or failed with the differences, in work/tests/<run>/ and the Self-test History, then clean its test data away (EAX_TEST_KEEP_DATA keeps it). It changes and restores the installation it runs on, so it asks first (`--yes` to skip), refuses on a server unless `--allow-server`, and takes a safety backup before it starts and puts it back at the end. Not in the web (restores stop the services the web runs on); the Backups page links to the command. When it passes, it proves the web's buttons too (R2.62). To discuss before building: whether it runs in a separate throwaway installation instead of the one in use, and how long a full run may take **Added 13 Nov 2026, 16:31 (owner: "in R3 we will do a full testing and detailed tests of this. full test should also test backup and restores when run from command line")**: `eax test full`, when run from the command line, includes this backup-and-restore test (back up, change, restore, check all the content); a full test started from the web leaves it out and says to run it from the command line **Decided (owner, 13 Nov 2026, 21:22 to 21:31)**: it runs on the **installation in use** (a safety backup first, put back at the end, and it asks before starting); **no time limit**. |

Steps: reproduce on Windows with Docker Desktop and on Linux; log what `_me()` sees; find the installation folder a sturdier way (the Compose labels `com.docker.compose.project.working_dir` and `config_files` on the helper, with the mount as a fallback, and a clear message naming the fix when neither is there); recreate the helper when its mounts are out of date at `up`; then a full update test from 1.0.0 to the next release on the laptop and a server (backup, maintenance page, build, start, health check, roll back), added to the self-test container (R2.3) **12 Nov 2026 (R2.0.1): the lookup made sturdier, to be checked on the laptop**: the helper finds its own container by id or by its Compose label, and the installation folder from any of the files mounted into it (the compose file, deploy/, eaxcli/, work/, version.toml) or Compose's working_dir label (a Windows path as Docker Desktop sees it); when none is there the message lists what it has and says to run eax up **Decided (owner, 13 Nov 2026, 21:22 to 21:31)**: the update from 1.0.0 to 2.0.0 tested on the **laptop (Windows) and a Linux server**, from System Admin › Updates and from `eax update`: backup, maintenance page, build, start, health check, roll back. |

Tests: every route answered by the API, none by a missing container. **Built 14 Nov 2026** (owner, after the smoke test was measured: "Just take from smoke and do it"): the two containers are gone from docker-compose.yml; the API (EAX_SERVE from EAX_API_SERVE, `all` unless set) registers their routes and answers /api/v1/operations/health and /api/v1/me/health itself (service_split.parts_served); service_split.part_url sends a part to the API unless EAX_OPERATIONS_INTERNAL_URL or EAX_PERSONAL_INTERNAL_URL names a container of its own; the front door's EAX_OPERATIONS_UPSTREAM and EAX_PERSONAL_UPSTREAM are `api:8000` unless set; health, start-up status and the estate diagrams name the API; the entrypoint still accepts EAX_SERVE=operations or personal, so a part can run alone again by configuration only. Saving measured in the smoke test: about 0.35 GB (the two containers). The role `api`/`job` split and copies (point 3) are left for when a server needs them. Tests: test_service_split.py |

| R3.5-M8 | **Release 3.5: resource history, recorded all the time and kept** (owner, 14 Nov 2026, 17:14: "add a story for this phase, to instrument memory CPU disk cache etc every 15 min configurable from admin screen and have it available and can be deleted. We should be able to run this while testing or working and keeping it"). Builds on M1's `eax footprint` (one light `docker stats` call, D620), but always on, not a watch started by hand: (1) **what is recorded** at each sample, per container and for the whole installation: memory (used, limit once M5 sets one), CPU (%), disk (each volume and the work folder, the images, free space on the Docker host), the Docker host's memory left and swap (paging), and the caches: the database's (PostgreSQL buffer cache hit rate, its size and connections), Redis or the job broker's memory where one runs, and the API's own caches; network in and out per container; (2) **every 15 minutes by default, the interval set on System Admin › Footprint** (for example 1, 5, 15 or 60 minutes, or off), applied without a restart; a shorter interval can be set for a while (during a test) and goes back by itself; (3) sampled by the operations helper (`ops`, which already reads Docker), so it runs **while people work and while the self-test runs**, and costs almost nothing (one call per sample); a sample is marked with what was happening (a self-test run and its level, a restore, a backup, an import, a start-up), so the figures under test and under use are told apart; (4) **kept** in the operations schema (D630, its own connection string; no logic or triggers in the database, D248), for as long as a setting says (90 days unless set), older samples summarised to one an hour rather than lost; (5) **available**: System Admin › Footprint shows charts over a chosen period (memory, CPU, disk, cache, per container or the whole), the peaks marked, a period compared with another (before and after a change, as Release 3.5 needs); downloadable as CSV; `eax footprint --history [days]` on the command line; R3.5-M1's watches and the self-test runs' memory tables read from the same history; (6) **can be deleted**: a period, everything before a date, or all of it, by a Super Admin, asked first and audited; the retention setting removes old samples by itself (housekeeping, R2.27); (7) R3.5-M5's alerts and R4's server estimate (R4.10) read their figures from it. Tests: sampling at the set interval, a changed interval taking effect, a sample during a self-test marked so, kept and summarised past retention, deleting a period, the CSV. **Built 14 Nov 2026** (owner, 17:17: "please build M8 and then we will close the phase"; D679): the job eax.resource_sample runs each minute and takes a sample when one is due; the operations helper's GET /resources (footprint.resources: one `docker stats` and one `docker ps`, the volume sizes from its five-minute background refresh) gives each container's memory, its limit, CPU, network and disk in and out and its volumes, the Docker host's memory, swap and processors, the file systems and what was happening (a self-test and its level, a backup, a restore, a start-up: a container still starting or the API up under ten minutes); the API adds the repository database's cache hit rate, size, connections and shared buffers and keeps it in operations.resource_samples (migration 0073; JSON per sample, no logic in the database); settings resource_every_minutes (15; 0 off), resource_detail_days (7, then one row an hour, average and peak) and resource_keep_days (90), on Settings › System and on System Admin › Footprint, with a shorter interval for a while (1, 2 or 5 minutes for 1 to 24 hours) that goes back by itself; System Admin › Footprint charts a period (6 hours to 90 days, or from and to) for the whole installation (memory average and peak, CPU as a share of the host, the host's memory left and swap, the database's cache hit rate between samples, the fullest disk) and each container, a self-test, backup, restore or start-up shaded, at most 400 points (more merged, peaks kept), a table of each container's average and peak, CSV, Sample now, and Delete (Super Admin: the period shown, everything before a date, or all; asked first, audited). `eax footprint --history [days]` prints the same summary. Not built: the API's own caches (none worth measuring) and an import marked as an activity. Tests: test_resources.py |

(1) **every report opens**: each dashboard in Reports (the ones imported at start-up, library/reports) opens as each role that may see it, its charts load without an error, and it is refused to a role that may not; (2) **with the run's own data**: a report opened for the run's model shows that model's content (the counts of its elements, relationships and views match what the run made), and a change made in the run (a new element, committed) shows after a refresh; uncommitted content never shows; (3) **from the screens**: a model's and a project's Reports tab opens a report in Reports for that model, signed in as the same person (single sign-on), and Back returns; (4) **the files reports make**: each download or export a report offers (Excel, Word, PDF, CSV) gives a non-empty file that opens, with the model's name and the run's content in it; the project documents made from templates as well; (5) **reports stay right**: a report deleted in Reports comes back at the next start (fixed 13 Nov 2026), a report changed by its owner is kept, and the import at start-up reports what it imported, skipped and failed; (6) **slow and missing**: Reports switched off or still starting is said on the page and skipped with the reason, never a failure of EA Explorer; a report that takes longer than a limit is a warning with its time. In the full self-test (a "reports" part, like backup), with a line per report in the results and the report's screenshot when it fails. To discuss before building: which reports count as critical, and the time limit **Decided (owner, 13 Nov 2026, 21:40)**: every report is critical (one that does not open, or shows the wrong data, makes the run Not healthy); a report slower than 30 seconds to open is a warning with its time, and one that has not opened after 60 seconds a failure. **Built 14 Nov 2026** (owner, 18:03: "add more test cases for reports then we will do a full test"; 18:05: "Browser with test users"; D681): the self-test's seventh suite, **reports** (part of full; `eax test reports` alone; eax/testing/reports_check.py; migration 0075 lets runs and results say so): Keycloak test users made for the run with the installation's own Keycloak administrator (a Super Admin, a Report Viewer and an IT Admin, named as the run's service accounts so EA Explorer's model access is theirs), each signed in to Reports through Keycloak in headless Chromium, then deleted from Keycloak and Superset at the end (a stopped run's, older than a day, by the next run): (1) every report dashboard opens as the Super Admin and as the Report Viewer, judged by every chart query Superset answers (an error a failure; slower than 30 seconds a warning; not loaded after 60 a failure; the screenshot kept); every report EA Explorer makes is there; the IT Admin sees no report; (2) the Report Viewer's charts show only models they have and the run's own; an element committed in the run shows, one left uncommitted never does; (6) Reports switched off or not up is skipped with the reason. Then (owner, 18:33: "Do 1, 2 and 3"): (3) a model's Reports tab (its API) links every report to Reports and one link opened lands on the report signed in as the same person; (4) the model's quality report as PDF and Excel and its tree as Excel, as the Report Viewer, each a file that opens, the workbooks naming the model; (5) Reports' log says what the import at start-up did, none failed; a report deleted in Reports coming back at the next start is not automated (it needs Reports restarted) and is said as a skipped check. Superset's own downloads (CSV, PDF of a dashboard) are not checked: the reports keep no saved query for them and the image has no browser for PDFs. Tests: test_reports_check.py |

| R4.11 | **Release 4 bug: the `eax` screen quits to the command prompt after each command** (owner, 14 Nov 2026, 08:13: "the eax screen mode after doing anything just quits and defaults to command prompt"). Meant (R3.1, D669): a command chosen from a pull-down runs either in a window inside the screen (its output shown, Close goes back) or, for one that needs the terminal, on the user screen with "Press Enter to return to the screen"; the screen stays until File › Exit or Alt+X. Seen on the owner's Windows laptop: after any command the screen closes and the PowerShell prompt is back. To find which of these it is, on Windows Terminal and the older console: (a) Textual's suspend (the user screen) not working on that console, so the app ends instead of coming back; (b) the command started through eax.ps1 ending the PowerShell session that started the screen (an `exit` in the script rather than a return code); (c) the command's own console input or Ctrl+C reaching the screen; (d) an error inside the screen after the command, hidden as it closes. To do: the screen comes back after every command, whichever way it ran, with the command's exit code shown; a command that fails says why and the screen stays; anything the screen cannot do on that console falls back to running the command in the window; an error inside the screen is written to work/logs/eax-screen.log and shown, not a silent exit. Tests: after a command in the window and on the user screen the app is still running (Textual's test pilot), the eax.ps1 route of `screen` keeps its loop (a test that fails without the fix, D599), and the Windows check run on the owner's laptop. Bugs come before features, so first in Release 4 **Fixed 14 Nov 2026** (owner, 18:33: "Do 1, 2 and 3"): the screen now only ends on Exit (Alt+X, File › Exit) or Ctrl+C; if it ends any other way (an error inside it, a console that dropped it after a command) why is written to work/logs/eax-screen.log, said, and the screen opens again; one that keeps closing within seconds (5 times running) gives way to the numbered menu; a command in the window runs with no console of its own on Windows (CREATE_NO_WINDOW), so it cannot change the console under the screen; a console that cannot hand the terminal over runs the command in the window instead. The Windows check still to run on the owner's laptop. Tests: test_r4_bugs.py (r4_11) |

| R4.13 | **Release 4 bug: a body that is not JSON is acted on, and the self-test archived its own model** (owner, 14 Nov 2026, 09:55: "Test execution is done please check the bugs and move them to R4"; found in the full self-test 20261114T061834Z: 2,099 passed, 16 failed, 8 warnings): POST /models/{id}/archive answered 200 to a body that is not JSON and to a list: the body is read as nothing, the state defaults to *archived*, and the model was archived in the background. That one call is why three journeys failed after it: drawn view as Model Admin and as Committer ("read only") and draw.io's Save as a drawn view (missing): the test model had been archived. Fix: (1) every endpoint that takes a JSON body refuses a body that is not JSON or not an object with 400, in one place (the shared request schema), never falling back to defaults; (2) archive and delete need their state said; (3) the negative tests never send to an endpoint that archives, deletes or restores (a list kept with the test), or send it to a model made for the purpose; (4) the three journeys run again and pass. Tests that fail without the fix (D599). Bugs come before features: first in Release 4 **Fixed 14 Nov 2026**: the cause was two routes for POST /models/<id>/archive: the phase-2 one (read only and hidden, no body) was registered first and took every call, so the screens' Archive only hid a model and a body that was not JSON archived it. It is removed (the read-only flag stays for MCP and the wizards, /reactivate brings such a model back); archiving a model or a project now needs {"state": "archived"|"deleted"} (422 otherwise, never a default); the negative tests never send anything to an archive, delete, restore, purge, discard, reset, revoke, clean or rollback endpoint. Tests: test_r4_bugs.py (r4_13) |

Each answers 404 (or 422 for an id that is not one), with a test each, and the negative tests pass. Bugs come before features: first in Release 4 **Fixed 14 Nov 2026**: a project backup that is not there 404 (checked inside the error handling); a governance record id that is not one 404 before any query; the industry pack of a model that is not there 404. Tests: test_r4_bugs.py (r4_14) |

| R4.19 | **Release 4: OpenBao on the laptop from `eax init`, so the secrets work** (owner, 14 Nov 2026, 10:47: "In R4 create a story for laptop mode init to have OpenBao, we need the secrets working"). D218 (6 Nov 2026) chose the bundled **OpenBao** (open source, MPL-2.0) as the vault, sealed with keys IT holds, every read audited, containers getting secrets as files at start, never as environment variables; what was built (D504) keeps the passwords in work/eaexplorer.env and copies them to a file, AWS or Azure (`eax secrets push/pull`), OpenBao not among them, so on a laptop every secret is in the config file. To build: (1) **`eax init laptop` sets up OpenBao** as a container of its own (its data in a volume, kept in backups), initialised and unsealed on the laptop without a person (the unseal key and the root token kept in work/ readable by the owner only, as a laptop has no IT team; a server keeps them with IT, D218), and **every secret generated there**: the database logins, Keycloak's, the reports' secret key, the broker password, the operations helper's token, the break-glass password; (2) the config file holds **references**, not values; `eax up` reads the vault and the containers get each secret **as a file** (Docker secrets or a tmpfs mount), never in their environment or on disk in clear; (3) **`openbao:` as a provider** of `eax secrets` (show, push, pull, rotate one value, which restarts what uses it), beside file, aws and azure; (4) the secrets EA Explorer itself reads by name come from the vault too: discovery's **EAX_SOURCE_...** sign-ins and the AI engine's **EAX_AI_...** keys (R3, D671), webhooks' signing keys; (5) upgrading an existing laptop: `eax secrets migrate` moves the config file's values into OpenBao (a dated copy first) and leaves references; (6) backups and restores carry the vault (sealed), a restore unseals it; the self-test checks the vault is unsealed, every reference resolves, no secret is in a container's environment (docker inspect) or the config file, and every read is in the vault's audit log; (7) its memory counted in Release 3.5's figures. **Discussed** (owner, 14 Nov 2026, 19:4x): a laptop **unseals by itself**; secrets reach containers as **Docker secrets**; a server **the same as a laptop** ("Server also keeps unseal keys like laptop non manually puts them"). **Part 1 built 14 Nov 2026** (D684): OpenBao 2.7.2 as the container `openbao` (profile vault, its store and audit log in the volume eax-vault, deploy/openbao/openbao.hcl, port 127.0.0.1:EAX_VAULT_PORT only); `eax init` (laptop and server; `--no-vault` for the old way) writes every password and key as a reference (KEY=openbao:KEY) and EAX_SECRETS_FROM=openbao:eax/eaexplorer; the first `eax up` starts the vault, initialises it (one unseal key and the root token in **persist/vault/keys.json**, owner only, never deleted by any reset or System Admin > Persist), **makes every value there** (40 hex characters each) and unseals it by itself at each start; eax (all three forms: eaxcli/vault.py, and eax.sh and eax.ps1 through `eaxpy secrets env` over a pipe) reads the values into its own environment before compose makes containers, never to disk; every service that reads the config file takes them through the compose anchor x-secrets; `eax secrets` show, migrate (an existing installation: values into OpenBao, references left), pull (back to clear), rotate KEY (a database login changed in the database too, over psql's input), set KEY, new; the console's password changes write into the vault; backups carry eax-vault (sealed) and persist/ (its keys), restores put both back and the operations helper unseals it; the health page and the self-test (security suite) check it is unsealed, every reference has a value, no password is left in the config file in clear, the audit log is on, and say which containers still hold a password in their environment; a reference reaching a container stops the setup. **Part 2, not built**: Docker secrets, each container reading its passwords from /run/secrets instead of its environment (PostgreSQL by *_FILE, our own services by a loader, Keycloak, Superset, Mayan and the broker each by its own means). It needs each image tried on Docker, so it waits for the owner's test of part 1. Tests: tests/test_vault.py (a stand-in OpenBao answering as its API does). Its memory (about 0.05 GB) is counted with the next `eax measure`. **Switched off** (owner, 14 Nov 2026, 20:34: "For now disable openbao by default. No installation can use it. All passwords go back in env file. I want to think about this."): D684; then (20:36) "there should be no way or config for users to turn on openbao until I give details": the container, its volume, network, backup set and settings are taken out; the code stays, off in the code itself; part 2 waits too. **Moved to Release 7, now Release 6** (owner, 14 Nov 2026, 21:56: "Add a new release for KeyVault OpenBao I will discuss in detail and then we will bring it in"; 15 Nov 2026: "Swap 6 and 7"): R6.1 to R6.3 |

One change from the story: no new MCP level (owner: "no new UI"); a machine credential needs read-write on MCP and its owner the Super Admin or IT Admin role. Restoring a single model or project stays on its page (one model's backups, D233). Tests: tests/test_mcp_operations.py |

| R5.1 | **Built 15 Nov 2026, released in 5.0.0 (D696; D697; eax/optimise.py, System Admin › Optimisation, eax optimise)**. **Release 5 epic: optimisations, measured and suggested in a report** (owner, 14 Nov 2026, 11:02: "Here we will measure the platform and suggest optimizations to the user in a report. These include CPU, mem size of server, deleting unused projects, archiving unused projects based on last run. Delete things from disk. Delete history of data since last tag and so on"). EA Explorer measures itself and writes an **Optimisation report**: each suggestion with its evidence, what it saves, its risk, how to undo it, and an **Apply** button for those allowed; **nothing is changed without a person's yes**, every apply runs as a background task with a backup first and is in the audit trail. On demand (System Admin › Optimisation, `eax optimise --report`) and on a schedule (monthly unless set), the newest report kept and compared with the last; the Super Admins and IT Admins told when a new one has suggestions. **What it looks at**: (1) **the server**: CPU, memory and disk used against what the server has, over weeks (the operations metrics, Release 3.5's measuring, R4.10's estimate): the size it needs, each container's memory limit and the number of api and job copies (D677), paging; (2) **unused projects and models**: last opened, changed, run or reported on; suggest **archiving** those unused for a set time (the archive, R2) and **deleting** archived ones past a further time (a Super Admin's yes, D-archive rules kept); (3) **the disk**: backups past their retention, logs, self-test results and recordings, footprint files, old exports, imports and deliveries, files no row refers to any more (orphans in Documents and the library), Docker images and build cache left by updates; (4) **history**: a model's change history before its last tag condensed or removed (the tag and everything after kept, so compare, undo and audit still work from the tag), only where the owner of the model agrees; the change log's retention; (5) **the database**: tables and indexes that have grown (bloat) to tidy, indexes never used, the slowest queries; (6) **the bought-in services**: the Reports cache, Documents' versions and search index, Keycloak's old sessions and events. Each suggestion is one line in the report with a tick to apply several together; a dry run shows exactly what would go first. Rules: the archive and tags are never removed by a suggestion, deleting is always a separate, confirmed step, and history condensing never touches anything after the last tag (D289 still holds for the screens: nothing is taken from them). **To discuss with the owner before building, one question at a time**: the time a project or model counts as unused, and by what (opened, changed, run); whether history before a tag is condensed or removed; which suggestions may apply on a schedule without asking (if any); the report's format (screen, PDF, Excel). Not built until the owner says |
| R5.2 | **Built 15 Nov 2026, released in 5.0.0 (D696; D686; services/ops/allowlist.py, System Admin › Security, eax allow)**. **Release 5: IP allow-listing** (owner, 14 Nov 2026, 20:13: "Add a new story IP white listing MFA IN NEXT release"). Only the addresses an organisation names reach EA Explorer, enforced at the front door (the proxy, nginx allow/deny before anything else answers): (1) **lists** of addresses and ranges (IPv4 and IPv6, CIDR), each with a name and why; (2) **where each applies**: the whole site, the sign-in pages, System Admin, the API for machine credentials (D366, its own port), MCP (/mcp), Reports and Documents; (3) kept in the config file (EAX_ALLOW_...), set on System Admin by a Super Admin, audited, a dated copy first, taken by the proxy without a restart; (4) **never a lock-out**: a change that would refuse the address of the person making it is refused, the laptop (127.0.0.1) and the Docker networks are always allowed, and `eax allow` on the machine itself clears or sets the lists when no screen can be reached; (5) the address seen is the real one behind a load balancer only when the load balancer is named as trusted (X-Forwarded-For from it alone); (6) each refusal counted and logged (address, where, when), shown on System Admin with the most refused addresses; a machine credential can also be limited to its own addresses. Tests: allowed and refused addresses at each place, the lock-out guard, a forwarded address from an untrusted source ignored, the self-test from inside still reaching everything. **To discuss with the owner before building, one question at a time**: which places need it first; a server only, or a laptop too; per role as well as per place. Not built until the owner says | **Decided** (owner, 15 Nov 2026, 11:07: "I assume 5.2 ip white listing will be managed on nginx", D686): yes, in the front door's nginx (allow and deny from a file the API writes and nginx reloads), as above.

| R5.4 | **Built 15 Nov 2026, released in 5.0.0 (D696; D687; eaxcli/datafolders.py, eax folders)**. **Release 5: where the data lives, set before Docker starts** (owner, 14 Nov 2026, 20:22: "add a story for next release to define mount points of data for DB's (all) for documents for logs for backups In a seperate location before docker is run and kept there for ensuring files are not on the same machine"). Today some places can already be moved one by one in the config file (EAX_DATA_DIR for the database, EAX_DOCS_DIR for documents, EAX_LOGS_DIR, EAX_BACKUP_DIR asked by `eax init server`, R2.44, D635), but nothing checks them, a laptop keeps them all in Docker volumes and work/, and the rest stay on the machine. To build: (1) **every place named, before Docker runs**: the databases (all of them on the main server: eaexplorer with its schemas, Keycloak's, Superset's, Mayan's, the library's, the views'), the documents' files, the logs, the backups (and with them the import and export folders, the certificate, persist/ and the key vault's store, R4.19), each a folder or a mounted share, kept in the config file; (2) **asked by `eax init`** (server: each one asked, with a sensible default on a mounted disk; laptop: optional, the Docker volumes as now) and **checked by every `eax up` before a container starts**: the folder is there, mounted (not the machine's own disk where a mount is wanted), writable by the container's user, with space to spare; `eax up` refuses to start and says why when one is not, so data never lands on the machine by accident; (3) **not on the same machine**: each place says whether it must be off this machine (a network share, a mounted volume, another disk), and the check refuses a place on the same file system as the operating system when it must be off it; the backups never on the same disk as the databases; (4) **kept there**: the containers use those folders only (bind mounts, no Docker volume for data), a restore and an update write back to them, and a move from the current place (`eax data move <what> <to>`) copies, checks and switches with the installation stopped, a dated copy of the config file first; (5) **shown**: System Admin > Footprint and the Health page list each place, where it is, whether it is mounted, its size and space left (R3.5-M8 records it); the self-test checks every place is where the config file says. Tests: a missing, unmounted, read-only or full place refuses `eax up` with its reason; the backups refused on the databases' disk; every service's data in its named place; a move keeps every file. **To discuss with the owner before building, one question at a time**: which places must be off the machine on a server (all, or the databases and backups first); network shares (SMB, NFS) or disks the operating system mounts before Docker; whether a laptop asks too. Not built until the owner says | **Decided** (owner, 15 Nov 2026, 11:07: "we just need to have a base folder's we put in then users can mount whatever they want if they want for example DB wont live on NAS but logs or docuemnts may", D687): simpler than above. **One base folder** (EAX_DATA_ROOT, asked by `eax init`) with a fixed folder under it for each kind: databases, documents, logs, backups, imports and exports, certificates; every container's data goes there and nowhere else. People mount whatever they want onto any of those folders (e.g. the logs and documents on a NAS, the databases on the local disk); EA Explorer does not decide what goes where. `eax up` checks each folder exists and is writable, and says which are on the machine's own disk and which are mounts; it does not refuse a start because of where a folder is. A laptop keeps today's Docker volumes unless a base folder is set.
| R5.6 | **Built 15 Nov 2026, released in 5.0.0 (D696; D688; System Admin › Logs)**. **Release 5: logs, levels and the web log, built with R5.7** (owner, 14 Nov 2026, 21:52: "Keep Logs - Logs at different levels debug Info Error and So on. We will by default keep it at an info level; Log rotation and deletion; Web log - What api was called, by who, response time, response status; Web log debug (off by default) - will also show input and output; a page to see these logs and download them; The page should clean them up; Logs get cleaned in clean-run; Logs are deleted once every 7 days in schedule"). Builds on R2.30 to R2.32 (logs on disk, one file a container a day, backed up, viewed and downloaded on System Admin, cleaned nightly) and changes three of their rules. To build: (1) **levels**: debug, info, warning, error and critical, for every container of ours (api, jobs, library, views, ops, setup, the self-test) and set for the others where they allow it (Keycloak, Superset, Mayan, nginx, PostgreSQL); **info by default on a laptop and a server alike** (R2.30 had debug on a laptop and error on a server); changed on System Admin › Settings or in the config file (EAX_LOG_LEVEL, and per container), taken without a restart where the container allows it, audited; (2) **rotation and deletion**: a new file each day and when one passes a size (set, 50 MB by default), compressed when closed; (3) **the web log**: one line for every API call (the API, the library, the views service, MCP): when, who (the person, or the machine credential and its owner), from which address, the method and path, the status, how long it took, how many bytes, the request id; written beside the container logs (logs/web/, one file a day), never with a password, token or cookie in it; (4) **web log debug** (off by default; switched on for a time, e.g. an hour, by a Super Admin, audited, said in the header while on): the request's input and the answer too, cut to a size, with every password, secret, token, key and personal field masked; (5) **a page**: System Admin › Logs gets a Web log tab beside the container logs: filter by time, person, path, status (errors only), slowest first; the slowest and most failing calls summed; download a period as CSV or the raw files as a zip; (6) **cleaning from the page**: delete a period, everything before a date or all of it (Super Admin, audited, a log backup first, D625); (7) **clean-run and clean-restart clean the logs** too (a change to R2.32, where they survived every reset: a log backup is taken first, so the last run's logs can still be read); (8) **every 7 days by schedule**: logs and web logs older than 7 days are deleted (the default; System Admin › Settings, "Delete logs older than"; R2.30's nightly job, 14 days on a laptop and 90 on a server until now); the log backups follow the backups' own keeping. Tests: the level set takes effect and info is the default; a file rotates at its size; every API call gives one web log line with its person, status and time; debug off has no input or output, debug on masks every secret; the page filters, downloads and deletes (audited); clean-run empties the logs after backing them up; the schedule removes what is older than 7 days and nothing newer. **To discuss with the owner before building, one question at a time**: whether the 7 days applies to a server too (audits may want longer); the web log's size limit and whether it goes to the database for the page or stays in files; which personal fields web log debug masks.
