| R5.9 | **Built 15 Nov 2026, released in 5.0.0 (D696; D689; eax/alerts.py, System Admin › Alerts)**. **Release 5: an alerts system, light (Nagios-like checks, not Nagios)** (owner, 14 Nov 2026, 21:58: "We need an alerts system like nagios please add a story for it"; 22:02: "I am not bought into ELK nagios we can discuss light weight tools"). Today alerts are pieces: the container watcher (R2.19: a container down or unhealthy, notifications, webhooks, the banner, optional restart), host levels (R2.16), the admin sign-in check (D215), the health page, Prometheus metrics at /api/metrics, the resource history (R3.5-M8). To build one alerts system over all of them: (1) **checks**, each on a schedule (every minute by default) with a state of OK, WARNING, CRITICAL or UNKNOWN as Nagios has: each service's health and response time, each container (up, healthy, restarts, memory near its limit), the hosts (CPU, memory, swap, disk and inodes), the database (connections, slow queries, size, cache hit rate, replication when there is a standby), the job queue (waiting, running long, failing), backups (the last good one too old, a failed one, the backup folder not mounted or full), the certificate (days to expiry), sign-in (Keycloak up, refusals rising), the web log's errors and slow calls (R5.6), the self-test's last run, the log store (R5.7), and checks a person adds (an address answers, a query returns under a level); (2) **thresholds** for warning and critical, set per check on System Admin; a state counted only after it holds for a number of checks (no alert for one slow answer); flapping noticed and said once; (3) **acknowledge, silence and downtime**: an alert acknowledged (with a note) stops repeating; a planned downtime (an update, a restore, the self-test) silences what it covers, and EA Explorer's own maintenance sets one by itself; (4) **notifying**: EA Explorer's notifications to IT Admins and Super Admins (who gets what, by check and level), webhooks (to a chat channel or a ticket system), the banner for critical ones; repeated at an interval until acknowledged; escalated to Super Admins when not acknowledged in time; recovery said too; never email unless the owner decides it (EA Explorer sends no email today); (5) **a page**: System Admin › Alerts: every check with its state, since when and its history, the open alerts first, acknowledge and silence there; a summary on the Health page and in the header; the history kept 90 days (R3.5-M8's rules), downloadable; MCP tools to read them (R4.22); (6) **a light tool or our own**: EA Explorer's own watcher grown (no new container), or a small open-source monitor as an optional service (for example Gatus, one small program set by a file; Uptime Kuma, with its own page; Prometheus with Alertmanager only if the metrics are wanted too), fed by EA Explorer's health and metrics; heavy suites (Nagios, Zabbix, Icinga) are out unless the owner says otherwise; either way the estate diagrams updated (R5.8). Tests: each check reaches warning and critical at its thresholds and back to OK; a short blip gives no alert; acknowledge stops the repeats, downtime silences, escalation and recovery are sent; notifications and webhooks carry the check, the level and a link; the page and the MCP tool show the same. **To discuss with the owner before building, one question at a time**: a light tool or EA Explorer's own (with the memory each takes, measured); who is told of what and how (chat channels, tickets, email); which checks first; whether a laptop runs it. Not built until the owner says |
| R5.10 | **Built 15 Nov 2026, released in 5.0.0 (D696; D685; eax sign, eax verify)**. **Release 5: signed deployments** (owner, 14 Nov 2026, 22:14: "add a story to sign deployments with keys"). Today `eax update`, `eax deploy`, eax-deploy.cmd and eax-deploy.sh fetch a release (a tag's archive or git, D417, D502) and build its images, and nothing proves the release is the one the owner made: no signature or checksum is checked. To build: (1) **each release signed** when it is made (docs/release-process.md): its archive and a manifest listing every file and image with its SHA-256, signed with the release key; the git tag signed too; our images (api, web, ops, backup, library, views, drawio, superset, testrunner) signed when they are built and pushed, and the third-party images pinned by digest, not tag; (2) **the keys**: a release signing key held by the owner, never in the repository or on an installation (offline, or in a hardware key); its public key shipped in the repository and in each installation (deploy/keys/), with a second key kept aside to replace it, and a way to retire one (a key list signed by the other); (3) **checked before anything runs**: `eax update`, `eax deploy`, the deploy page (D555) and the eax-deploy scripts verify the signature and every checksum before unpacking or building, and refuse a release that does not verify, saying why; the version check (R4.23) and the update channels (D417) show whether a release is signed; (4) **no way round it by mistake**: an unsigned or failing release is refused; only a Super Admin can override, typed in full and audited, for a build of their own (a laptop building from its own checkout is not affected: it is the person's own code); (5) **shown**: System Admin › Updates and the About page say the running release, its signature and who signed it; the self-test checks the running files match the signed manifest (nothing changed on the machine since). Tests: a signed release verifies and installs; a changed file, a wrong key, a missing signature and a retired key are each refused with their reason; the override is audited; the manifest check finds a file changed after install. **To discuss with the owner before building, one question at a time**: which signing tool (for example minisign or SSH signatures for the archive, Sigstore cosign for the images, or GPG for all); where the private key lives; whether images are published to a registry or built on each machine as now. Not built until the owner says | **Decided** (owner, 15 Nov 2026, 11:07: "we need a command in eax so that consumers can sign deployments if they want we wont", D685): **we do not sign releases**; what is above is replaced by a command for the organisations that run EA Explorer: `eax sign` makes their own key pair (the private key kept by them, outside the installation), signs a release or deployment they have checked (the archive and a manifest of every file and image with its SHA-256), and `eax verify` checks one; when an installation has a trusted public key (deploy/keys/), `eax update`, `eax deploy` and the deploy page check the signature before unpacking and refuse a release that does not verify; with no key, nothing changes (signing is optional). All three forms of eax (eax.ps1, eax.sh, eaxcli) and help.txt.

To build: (1) **a service catalogue**: the things people can ask for, each with its own form (the fields it needs), who works on it (a role or a group, e.g. Model Creators, Super Admins, a project's admins), whether it needs an approval first and by whom, and how long it should take (a target); (2) **tickets**: raised by anyone signed in from the catalogue (a menu item and the start page); each with a number (e.g. REQ-123), a status (new, waiting for approval, approved, in progress, waiting for the requester, done, declined, cancelled), a priority, who raised it, who it is assigned to, comments both ways, attachments, a history of every change, and links to what it is about (a model, a project, an entity); (3) **queues and boards**: each team sees its queue (list and board by status), takes or assigns tickets, filters and searches; the requester sees "My requests"; targets shown and late ones marked; (4) **approvals**: a ticket that needs one waits for the approver (a Model Admin, a Project Admin, a Super Admin as the catalogue says), approved or declined with a note, audited; (5) **done from the ticket where it can be**: for a catalogue item that EA Explorer can carry out (a new model from a template, access to a model, a new project), the person working it opens the same wizard with the ticket's answers filled in, and the ticket is closed with a link to what was made; nothing is done without that person (or, later, an approved automatic rule); (6) **told**: EA Explorer's notifications at each step (raised, assigned, a comment, waiting for you, done), webhooks for each change (to a chat channel or another ticket system), no email unless the owner decides it; (7) **MCP**: tools to raise a ticket, list mine and a queue, comment and (for those who work them) move them, with the preview and confirm of D458 for changes (R4.22); (8) the person-to-person requests of phase 8.1 kept as they are, or folded in as a catalogue item "Ask a person" (to discuss); reports of tickets by item, team, time to close and late ones. Tests: a ticket from each kind of form; the right queue and approver; an approval needed before work; done from the ticket makes the model or access and links it; every change in the history and audited; who may see a ticket (the requester, the team, Super Admins only); notifications and webhooks sent; the MCP tools. **To discuss with the owner before building, one question at a time**: the service catalogue (its items, forms, teams, approvals and targets); whether the phase 8.1 requests fold in; whether tickets also come in from or go out to another ticket system. | R5.12 | **Built 15 Nov 2026, released in 5.0.0 (D696; D691; eax/dbmaint.py, System Admin › Database maintenance, eax db)**. **Release 5: database maintenance, by the application** (owner, 14 Nov 2026, 23:23: "Database mantainance, the application checkes the dB and does mantainance of the dB vaccine analysis clearing logs and so on Lock resolution etc"). Today System Admin › Operations › Database shows the largest tables, the slowest queries and when each table was last tidied (phase 35, operations.db_health), and housekeeping removes old rows by its rules (D371, D445); PostgreSQL's own autovacuum does the rest unseen. To build, for every database on the server (eaexplorer with its schemas, Keycloak's, Superset's, Mayan's, the library's, the views'): (1) **checks**, on a schedule and on demand: tables and indexes swollen with dead rows (bloat), tables autovacuum has not reached, statistics out of date, indexes never used or duplicated, transaction ID age (wraparound), connections near the limit, long transactions and sessions idle in a transaction, the cache hit rate, the size and its growth; each said in words with what it means and what will be done; (2) **maintenance**: VACUUM (ANALYZE) of the tables that need it, ANALYZE after a large load or restore, REINDEX CONCURRENTLY of a swollen index, VACUUM FULL only by a person (it locks the table), the logs and history the housekeeping rules keep cleared on time (R5.6's logs, task files, old notifications and webhook deliveries, D371); a window for the heavy work (at night by default, set on System Admin), never while a backup, a restore or the self-test runs; (3) **locks**: who blocks whom (pg_locks and pg_stat_activity: the blocking query, the person or job behind it, how long), shown live; a blocked call waits only up to a set time (lock_timeout and statement_timeout per part, so one stuck job never holds the screens); a session idle in a transaction too long is ended by itself (idle_in_transaction_session_timeout); ending a blocking session is offered to a Super Admin (cancel first, then terminate), audited; deadlocks counted and their queries kept; (4) **query times**: pg_stat_statements switched on (the database container's settings), so the slowest queries by total and by average are known, with a reset; (5) **a page**: System Admin › Operations › Database grows a Maintenance tab: every check's state, what was done and when (a history kept 90 days), what is due, the locks now, buttons to run a check or the maintenance now (Super Admin, audited); alerts on a check going critical (R5.9); MCP tools to read them and, with preview and confirm, to run maintenance or end a blocking session (D458, R4.22); (6) memory and disk: maintenance's own use measured (R3.5-M8 marks it as an activity), and the space it frees said. Tests: a table given dead rows is found and vacuumed; stale statistics are analysed; an unused index is listed (never dropped by itself); a lock held by one session is shown with its blocker, a timeout stops the waiting call, ending it is audited; nothing heavy runs during a backup, restore or self-test; every database is checked. **To discuss with the owner before building, one question at a time**: what runs by itself and what waits for a person; the maintenance window; whether the application may end a session by itself after a time. Not built until the owner says |

| R5.19 | **Built 15 Nov 2026, released in 5.0.0 (D696; eax/sizing.py, System Admin › Sizing, eax estimate)**. **Moved from R4.10** (Release 4 closed, owner, 15 Nov 2026, 07:18: "please add carried tiems from 4 to this release"). **Release 5: server sizing estimate** (owner, 14 Nov 2026, 07:40: "A user will put in the number of concurrent users and models and projects. We need to be able to predict RAM, CPU and Disk for server deployments"). A person enters **how many people at once** (concurrent users), **how many models** (and roughly how large: elements and relationships each, small, medium or large) and **how many projects** (with their documents), plus what they switch on (Reports, Documents, the MCP server, discovery, backups kept and for how long). EA Explorer answers with the **RAM, CPU cores and disk** a server needs: in total and per container (the API and its workers, the database, Keycloak, Reports, Documents, the front door and the rest), the disk split into the database, documents and files, Reports and backups (with growth over a year), and the settings to use (each container's memory limit, the API's workers, the database's memory settings). (1) **a best estimate, said to be one** (owner, 14 Nov 2026, 07:42: "R4.10 will have to be done without 4.9 based on best guess. We will not have server class machines to run this"): it does not wait for the benchmarks (R4.9). A sizing model in one file of its own, per container a fixed base plus what each concurrent user, model, element, project and document adds, worked out from: what each container uses on the owner's laptop today (R3.6's measuring, the operations metrics), each component's own published requirements (PostgreSQL, Keycloak, Superset, Mayan EDMS, Redis and the rest), the API's workers and connections per user, and the sizes of the data itself (rows and indexes per element, files per document, backups kept). Every answer lists its **assumptions** (per-user figures, data sizes, headroom) and labels itself an estimate; the assumptions can be changed on the screen and are kept with the result; later, figures from real installations (part 4) and from R4.9 if it runs correct the model, each correction a change to that one file with its reason; (2) **a margin** for peaks (a set headroom, 30% unless set) and the answer rounded to sizes a server comes in; one server or, past a size, the advice to split (the database or Reports on their own, R2.50); (3) **where**: System Admin › Deploy (and `eax deploy`) before a server is made, so the plan uses the figures, and `eax estimate --users N --models N --projects N` on the command line; the result can be saved or exported (PDF or Excel) for an infrastructure team; (4) **checked against reality**: on a running installation the estimate is shown beside what it really uses (the operations metrics, Hosts), so the model is corrected as real figures come in; the figures are refreshed when real figures or benchmarks come in; (5) **the same for containers** (owner, 14 Nov 2026, 07:41: "same for containers etc for them to estimate this out"): for a deployment on a container platform (Docker Swarm, Kubernetes, a cloud's container service) rather than one server, the estimate per container: its CPU and memory to reserve and its limit, how many copies (replicas) to run for the users entered and which containers can have more than one (the API, its workers, the front door) and which cannot (the database, the one-shot set-up containers), each volume's size and growth (the database, documents, Reports' home, backups) and the storage it needs, and the nodes (machines) those add up to with room to move a container when one node is down; exported as the settings to paste: a compose override with each service's limits and replicas, and the requests, limits and replicas for each container as a table a platform team turns into their own manifests; (6) tests: for a laptop-sized input the estimate comes out at the laptop's own settings today (within a tolerance), it grows as each input grows and never shrinks, the limits it suggests are ones the compose files accept, the replicas never exceed one for a container that cannot run twice, the per-container figures add up to the server total, and every answer carries its assumptions. Does not need R4.9. Not built until the owner says |

The API's log of the full test: **eax.industry_packs**, the 10-minute job that loads the industry packs into Documents, started at 17:50, 18:00, 18:20 and 18:30 UTC and never ended (18:10's found the loader's lock taken and left); its queueing_lock only stops a second run waiting, not a second one running, so a slow or blocked load (Documents still starting, each file allowed 5 minutes) let one run after another pile up until all four slots were theirs, and the backup asked for at 18:54 never got one. Fixed: a run that finds another loading leaves at once, before it reads or waits on anything; a run loads for at most 8 minutes and leaves the rest to the next (the start-up status says "N of M loaded; the rest in the next 10 minutes"); its database waits are short (a lock 30 s, a statement 2 minutes). With R5.30 the scheduled jobs no longer share slots with people's work at all. **The same job never runs twice at once** (owner, 22:01: "if a job already exists like we had exactly the 4 jobs of loading industry pack then can we ensure it doesnt load again?"): every scheduled job also carries a lock of its own name, so a second one waits, without taking a slot, until the first ends. **Nothing is killed without a person** (22:01: "I dont want any job to be killed without human approval, recomendation yes"): EA Explorer only recommends; Kill, Clear the stuck jobs and Restart the job runner are a person's click, confirmed. (A run of the industry packs ending itself after its 8 minutes, between two files, is not a kill: it stops cleanly and the next run carries on.) **The alert to kill and clear** (21:46): the alert jobs_stuck says which jobs and "Kill and clear them on System Admin › Jobs"; there and on the dashboard **Clear the stuck jobs** kills every stuck one and restarts the job runner, in one go |

| R5.36 | **Fixed 15 Nov 2026, released in 5.1.0 (eax/dbmaint.py end_idle; tests/test_r5_36_idle_sessions.py)**. **Bug: every 5 minutes every database connection on the server was ended** (owner, 15 Nov 2026, 22:25: "sample data doesnt seem to be loading and I cant see projects I can see models is this working ?", the start-up showing "Sample data loading 2 of 32"). The API's log: at 20:16:07, 20:20:25 and 20:25:04, as the job eax.db_idle_sessions (R5.12) ran, every connection ended with "terminating connection due to administrator command"; the sample data's loader died after the second of 32 models (the projects come later in it), the change log and the alerts lost theirs. The job's query put pg_terminate_backend(pid) in its WHERE, as a superuser: PostgreSQL may call it before testing the other conditions, so it ended every session of every database on the server (Keycloak's, Reports' and Documents' too), its own included. Very likely also behind the full test's stuck industry packs (Documents losing its database) and the Keycloak test users not deleted. Fixed: the sessions idle in a transaction too long are chosen first, in a query of their own, only client sessions of EA Explorer's own database, then ended one by one |

The loader held its one-at-a-time lock on a connection left idle in a transaction for the whole load; the start-up load has no 8-minute budget and ran for about an hour, so database maintenance (R5.12) ended the session after its 30 minutes and the unlock at the end failed, although every file had loaded. The lock's connection is now in autocommit (idle, never idle in a transaction), and a connection already gone is not an error (its lock went with it) |

A part served by the API is now checked where it runs (it reaches its schema, as its /health does); a part in a container of its own is still asked over HTTP |

| R5.45 | **Fixed 16 Nov 2026, released in 5.1.0 (eax/jobqueue.py _jobs_path, recover; services/api/entrypoint.sh; tests/test_r5_45_jobs_left_by_a_stopped_runner.py)**. **Release 5 bug: four scheduled jobs "being worked on" for 25 to 40 minutes, their next runs stuck behind them, and Kill and Cancel did nothing** (owner, 16 Nov 2026, 08:36, screenshots: "When repo is built and all is up why is this job still there? Killing is not really doing anything. Nor is cancelling"; Kill answered a server error, ProgrammingError). Two faults: (1) Kill, Cancel and Move changed a job's status without the jobs schema on the search path, and the job runner's own trigger names its types without their schema: "type procrastinate_job_status does not exist", nothing changed; now each sets the path for its own transaction; (2) the job runner was restarted at 06:12 (the API's log: "Stop requested", after the job slots were changed); the jobs it was running (project repositories, alert checks, resource sample, announce restores) ended with it but their rows stayed "doing" for ever, each holding its job's lock, so the next run of each waited for ever. Now, before the job runner starts, with one API server every job still "doing" belongs to no one and is marked stopped (failed, with why; nothing is killed: it had already stopped), so the next runs start; with several servers nothing is touched and Kill deals with a left-over one. People's work (the tasks queue, the self-test) was not held up |
| R5.46 | **Fixed 16 Nov 2026, released in 5.1.0 (eax/jobqueue.py judge, kill, delete_history; eax/testing/jobs_check.py; services/api/entrypoint.sh; migration 0090_selftest_jobs_suite; Jobs.jsx, TestRuns.jsx, selfTestDriver.js; eaxcli help and commands; tests/test_r5_46_jobs_review.py, tests/test_r5_46_jobs_on_a_real_database.py)**. **Release 5: the job queue's code reviewed, a Jobs item in the self-test** (owner, 16 Nov 2026, 08:41: "ensure if jobs are dead then they dont show up. Found and fixed: (1) a waiting job behind an earlier run of itself was called stuck: it now says which job it waits for; (2) a job put off to a later time counted as waiting from when it was asked for: it now says when it starts; (3) a job asked to stop was never judged; (4) Kill marked a job still running as ended, and when its work ended the job runner could not finish it and stopped: Kill now asks it to stop ("Being stopped"; Restart the job runner ends it, and the restart marks it stopped), a second Kill ("Mark stopped") marks it stopped at once for a job no runner has any more; (5) Delete history failed on the job runner's own trigger, like Kill (R5.45); (6) Cancel and Kill wrote their event twice; (7) only one of the three job runners was watched: the scheduled or the alerts runner could stop and nothing restarted it; now any of them stopping restarts the container, and the restart marks its jobs stopped (dead jobs no longer show as being worked on). **The Jobs suite** (Self-test, part of the full test, `eax test jobs`): the job runner takes jobs, the alert checks run, nothing stuck or left by a stopped runner, every scheduled job has its lock, every job has a name in words, and Cancel, Move and Kill work on probe jobs in a transaction rolled back. **Tests on the job runner's real tables and triggers** (PostgreSQL, EAX_JOBS_TEST_DSN; they caught (5)) besides the code tests |

| R5.5 | **Built 16 Nov 2026, released in 5.5.0 (D705; eax/discovery_projects.py, eax/mcp_discovery.py, eax/api/discovery_projects.py, DiscoveryProjects.jsx, migration 0094; tests/test_r5_5_discovery.py and test_r5_5_discovery_on_a_real_database.py)**. **Release 5.5 epic: enterprise discovery** (moved from Release 5, owner, 14 Nov 2026, 18:53: "Let's move enterprise diwcovery to R5.5"; kept at 5.5, 15 Nov 2026, 07:21: "swap 5.5 and 5.6"; first asked 09:13: "the ability to scan sources like repositories, file systems, video etc and using AI build metamodel from it"). Release 3's discovery reads feeds, takes pushes and reads documents into a model whose metamodel already exists; this goes further: **scan what the organisation has and let AI propose the metamodel itself**, and the content that fits it. (1) **Sources scanned**: code repositories (Git: the services and applications in them, their languages and frameworks, the APIs they offer and call, their dependencies, databases and schemas, the infrastructure as code, Dockerfiles, compose and Kubernetes files, pipelines); file systems and shares (documents, spreadsheets, presentations, diagrams in draw.io, Visio and the exchange format, PDFs); **video and audio** (meeting recordings and walkthroughs: transcribed, then read like a document, the moment in the recording kept with each finding); and later others of the same shape (wikis, ticket systems, cloud accounts); (2) **what AI proposes**: a **metamodel** (entity types, their attributes, relationship types and which types each may join) built from what was found, laid against a standard where one fits (ArchiMate 4, the TOGAF content metamodel) so a found "service" or "queue" lands on a known type, a new type only where nothing fits; and the **content** (elements and relationships) in that metamodel; (3) **reviewed, never applied blind**: the metamodel proposal arrives as a **metamodel draft** (the existing drafts: compared, changed, published by a Model Admin) and the content as **deliveries** (preview, accept, decline, as in Release 3), each finding with its **evidence** (the file and line, the page, the slide, the time in the video) and a confidence; near duplicates suggested as matches; (4) **scans that run where the data is**: a scanning agent (a container) that reads repositories and shares on the organisation's network, read only, limited to the paths and repositories listed, sending only findings (never whole files) to EA Explorer; on demand or on a schedule, and later only what changed since the last scan; (5) **safe with confidential material**: secrets found in code and files are never sent or kept (detected and left out); the AI engine is the admin's choice as in Release 3, a local engine for what may not leave the building; each source and scan says what it may read; every scan, finding and decision in the audit trail; limits on size and cost per scan; (6) **how it shows**: a model's Discovery gains *Scan* sources and a scan's results grouped by what was found (the proposed types, then the elements under each), with the evidence beside each, and a picture of the proposed metamodel before it is accepted. Builds on Release 3's discovery (D670 to D676): the AI engine, deliveries, matches, the wizards. **To discuss with the owner before building, one question at a time**: which sources first; whether a scan starts a new model or adds to one; how far AI may go on its own (propose only, or apply what it is sure of); the agent's deployment; the video and audio formats and languages. Not built until the owner says **The owner's design (15 Nov 2026, 20:11 to 20:27), which leads. Above all (20:27): "EA explorere in this use case should do minimum work which is providing resource list to MCP and a model. If MCP needs to login use other tools thats its problem. EA exporer will only be source and sink of information. Let MCP and LLm run this one"**: EA Explorer keeps the discovery projects, the list of resources each one covers and the target model, hands them to the AI through MCP, and takes back what the AI found (the metamodel changes, the content, where each came from, where each resource got to); it never signs in to a source, reads, extracts or transcribes anything itself: the AI client, with its own tools, reaches each resource, reads it (attachments, scanned pages and recordings included), and skips what is marked not for AI (told so by the tool, and reporting what it skipped). Read the points below in that light: (a) **a discovery project**, a project type of its own (not an ADM project): its name, its **target model** and its **sources**, its runs and its schedule; (a2) **a new global role, Discovery Admin** (eax-discovery-admin; owner, 20:22), given like the other global roles: sets up the sources with their sign-ins and the discovery projects, for any model; Super Admins can too; (b) **sources configured in the platform so they can be reached**: mailboxes, document folders, FTP and SFTP, SharePoint, and the others proposed and agreed ("add other sources as you suggested"): Git repositories, wikis (Confluence), ticket systems and the CMDB (Jira, ServiceNow), Teams and Slack channels, database schemas (read only), cloud storage (S3, Azure Blob, Google Drive, OneDrive), cloud accounts (AWS, Azure), recordings (transcribed); **all of them in Release 5.5** (owner, 20:26: "All of them in 5.5"), each source kind its own story within it; (c) **sign-in by keys, basic, client certificates and SSO** (OAuth, Microsoft 365 for SharePoint and mailboxes): **tested, then forgotten** (owner, 20:28: "EA explorer may keep ifnormation for signin to test the link after that it can forget"; "Test, then forget"): a Discovery Admin types the sign-in when adding a resource to Test the link; EA Explorer uses it once, says whether the resource answered, and keeps nothing but the method; at run time the AI client brings its own access; (d) **the AI reads everything it is given** ("let AI read everything. Onus is on the organization to share mailboxes"): no filtering by the platform beyond what each source is set to share; **attachments are read**, a setting of each source ("Ensure attachments are read in tool config"); **a file or attachment marked "NOT FOR AI USE OR AUTOMATED PROCESSING" is never given to the AI** (owner, 20:17: "respect it"): its title, place and date kept and listed in the run's report as skipped, marked not for AI; (e0) **what a run reads is the AI's choice, asked in the MCP tool** (owner, 20:23: "Let AI decide based on how it was setup. Please put this to be asked in mcp tool"): the run tool asks which: only what is new or changed since the last run, or everything again; the project's setup gives the default and the AI decides each time; the platform keeps where each source got to so "only new" works; **what is gone from a source is the AI's choice too, asked in the tool** (owner, 20:24): for an element whose source item is gone, the run tool asks whether to mark it (a status saying since which run it was not found, listed in the run's report) or delete it; the project's setup gives the default; the run's tag still allows compare and undo; (e) **EA Explorer is the source and sink, the AI does the rest, through MCP** ("We provide config, heavy lifting in the LLM using MCP"; 20:27): an MCP tool `run discovery project <name>` hands the AI the project's resources (each with its kind, where it is, what to include, whether to read attachments, and where the last run got to) and the target model's metamodel and content; the AI reaches and reads them with its own tools and sends back the metamodel changes and the elements and relationships found, each with where it was found, and where each resource got to; (e2) **every element and relationship the AI adds or changes keeps where it was found** (owner, 20:25: "Yes, always"): the source, the item (the mail, file, page, ticket or commit), where in it (the page, line or moment in a recording) and the run; shown on the element as "Found in", with a link to open the item where the person may; (e3) **things that may already be there** (owner, 20:28: "Let the AI decide, flag doubts"): the AI matches what it finds to the elements already in the model as best it can; where unsure it adds the element and records "may be the same as <element>", listed in the run's report for a person (the existing Dedupe merges them); (e4) **the AI is authenticated separately** (owner, 20:28: "AI will be authenticated seperately"): it reaches EA Explorer with its own MCP sign-in (an MCP token or machine credential of its own), apart from any source's sign-in and from the person's own; (f) **the AI commits into the project's target model** (owner, 20:15: "AI commits into target model onus is on the people who created the model to manage it to be a child or direct"): whoever sets up the project chooses the target; a child model of the main one when they want to review first (merged into its parent with the existing merge, reviewed, or directly once they trust the AI: "when we are confident about ai then we can do a direct merge"), or the main model itself; (f2) **the AI changes the metamodel and the content** (owner, 20:17: "AI can change metamodel and content. We need a mechanism to show what meta model entities have been changed. So everytime the AI starts it should tag the model and then update this way we can see what changed between tags"): **every run first tags the target model** (a tag named for the project and the run), then commits its metamodel changes and its content; what a run changed is the comparison of its tag with the next (the existing compare of two tags, which compares the metamodels item by item as well as the content), shown on the run's page, the metamodel changes first; (g) **the AI agent is the user** of these changes ("The user for these changes is ai agent"; 20:21: "AI agent on behalf of person. Clearly saying its AI agent on behalf of person who setup the Project"): every change, commit, tag and merge of a run is made by "AI agent on behalf of <the person who set up the discovery project>", shown so wherever an author is (the change log, history, compare, reports), the project and run recorded with it; whose AI client ran it kept in the run's record; (h) **the people of the project bring their own AI, and it is the AI that runs and schedules discovery** (owner, 20:20: "The user of the projects bring there own AI. Hence I feel this should be scheduled by the AI"; this replaces 20:14 "Let both schedule a run"): EA Explorer holds no AI key and calls no AI; a run starts only from an AI client through the MCP tool, by hand or on the client's own schedule. **The project keeps its schedule as a marker of when a run is due** (owner, 20:20: "yes"): set on the project (every day, week or month, a day and a time), seen by everyone on it; EA Explorer runs nothing, the AI client's scheduled task asks the MCP tool `discovery projects due` and runs those; a run past its time shows as overdue on the project (and in the alerts). Still to agree, one question at a time |
| R5.5.1 | **Built 16 Nov 2026, released in 5.5.0 (eax/decisions.py, GovernanceRecords.jsx, migration 0093; tests/test_r5_5_1_decisions.py and test_r5_5_1_decisions_on_a_real_database.py)**. **Release 5.5: architecture decision records and dispensations in the Governance Log** (owner, 15 Nov 2026, 18:20: "Please add this requirement for next phase", with a feature specification). Builds on the governance records projects already have (eax/governance.py: the kinds Decision and Dispensation, D318), making them first-class and linked: (1) **records**: an Architecture Decision Record (ADR) and a Dispensation (waiver), numbered per year (ADR-2026-001, DISP-2026-001), in the Governance Log of the organisation's repository and of every project, offered in every ADM project template; (2) **links both ways** to the project and its ADM phase (Preliminary, A to H, Requirements Management), to architecture principles and standards, to stakeholder requirements, to the affected model elements (applications, capabilities, technology components, data entities, value streams) and to other records (supersedes, superseded by, depends on); an element's page gets a **Linked decisions and dispensations** tab with the active records and the waivers coming to an end; (3) **lifecycle**: Draft, Proposed (Architecture Board review), Accepted, then Superseded, Deprecated, Rejected or Expired (a dispensation: Accepted as an active waiver, Expired, Closed or remediated); acceptance through the existing sign-off, the Board's approver, date and comments kept in the history and the change log; (4) **dispensations end, and people are told** (owner, 15 Nov 2026, 18:20: "Dispensations should trigger notifications", "on end date"): a review or expiry date is required; **on the end date a notification** (needing action) goes to the dispensation's owner, its sponsor, the project's Project Admins and Lead Architects and the Architecture Board, and it becomes Expired by itself (a daily job, D248); those ending within 30 days (a setting) are shown on the governance dashboard with a reminder beforehand; (5) **two templates shipped** in the library, each a form with its required fields: the ADR (title, record type, status, project and phase, governing principles, linked elements, context and problem, the options considered with their pros and cons, the decision and why, the consequences and trade-offs, related records, the owner, the Board's approval) and the dispensation (title, status, the principle or standard waived, the systems and projects, the justification and business impact, the risks and mitigations, the remediation plan, the expiry date, the sponsor's and the Board's sign-off); (6) **reporting**: a report view rpt.v_governance_decisions (the report login, read only) and its OData feed, test data left out as in every report view; one standard governance report: decisions by status and project, active dispensations by expiry date with those ending within 30 days marked; (7) sample data in the sample projects (D550) and the self-test's API and journey checks. Questions for the owner before it is built: whether a record can belong to the organisation without a project; who may accept (the Architecture Board's members, R4 board, or the project's Lead Architect); whether expired waivers raise an alert (R5.9). Not built until the owner says **Agreed with the owner (15 Nov 2026)**: (i) **the Architecture Board** (20:31: "Project sign-off, as today"): in a project, its existing sign-off people and quorum accept an ADR or a dispensation; in the organisation's own Governance Log, outside projects, a new global role **Architecture Board** (eax-architecture-board) does; (ii) **warned before the end** (20:32: "30 days before and on the day"): a notice 30 days before (a setting) and one needing action on the end date, when the dispensation becomes Expired; (iii) **the discovery AI may write them too, in any state** (20:33: "Yes, any state"): ADRs and dispensations it finds in what it reads (R5.5) are added or changed like any other content, accepted included, as "AI agent on behalf of <the person who set up the discovery project>" with Found in; (iv) **the two templates exactly as in the specification** (20:34: "As written"): the ADR's and the Dispensation's fields, shipped with the TOGAF 10 library; Super Admins may add fields later. Agreed; ready to build |

No other feature in this release but R5.6-Q2, job priorities (22:47), R5.6-U1, Updates reading GitHub Releases (23:22), and R5.6-U2, Deploy and Updates choosing the version from a drop-down of those releases (16 Nov, 07:39). R5.6-T1 (pausing a test run), R5.6-Q1 (fair turns on the job queue) and R5.6-Q3 (every job reviewed) were removed (owner, 16 Nov 2026, 15:21: "remove 5.6 T1, 5.6 Q1 R.6 Q3"; D708). | R5.6-B1 | **Built 16 Nov 2026, in 5.6.0 (D711, D712)**. **From R5.13** (owner, 15 Nov 2026, 07:20). **Release 5.6: backup and restore, tested and fixed** (owner, 15 Nov 2026, 07:07: "Add backup and restore testing to this release"). **The owner's test, which leads** (16 Nov 2026, 15:21): **(a) B1**: take an infrastructure backup of the installation as it is, called B1; **(b) make content**: an architecture project with several models; in them, content of every kind in various states: entities and relationships, committed and uncommitted changes, child models and merges, tags, drawn views, custom views, custom reports (report designs and KPIs), documents in the project (Documents, with their files), governance records, stakeholders, key questions and the rest of what a project holds; **(c) B2**: take a second infrastructure backup, called B2; **(d) restore B1**: the project and its models, and everything made in (b), are gone (checked, not assumed); **(e) restore B2**: everything comes back, and **all of it is checked**: every database's rows compared with what was there before B1 was restored, and **every file by its checksum** (documents, uploads, icons, the views' drawings, the config and persist/), sign-in, dashboards and documents opening. **Where it runs** (owner, 15:28, asked: "This installation, guarded"; 15:36: "If the enviornment is lost thats upto the user"): on the live installation itself, after one typed confirmation that says plainly that everything made since B1, real work too, is lost until B2 is restored; nothing else refuses it: what happens to the installation is the person's choice. **How it starts and runs: a test style of its own, synchronous** (owner, 15:30, asked: "Command line only"; 15:36: "This needs to be a seperate test style. It needs to be completly syncrhronous calling one comamnd after other within eax and calling the require apis. This will not be async"): `eax test backup-restore` is a script inside eax itself, not a self-test run: no test runner container, no background task, no job queue, nothing polled. It does one step after another and waits for each to end before the next: eax's own commands (`eax backup`, `eax restore`, waiting for the containers to be healthy) and the API calls it needs (making the project, models and content, reading them back), each step printed as it runs with its result, stopping at the first step that fails and saying which and why. At the end, a report file of every step, every comparison and every checksum (work/tests/backup-restore-<time>/), and the same in the log. **Afterwards** (owner, 15:31, asked: "Removed"): once everything matched, the test project and its models are deleted like any self-test data (named self_test_...). **The two backups** (owner, 15:41, which replaces 15:33: "The name is not B1 or B2 they should be randomly generated. At the end of the test all backups of B1 and B2 are removed."): B1 and B2 are only how this story speaks of them; each is named with a randomly generated name (and the test's report says which was which), and at the end of the test both are removed, with any backup a restore took first on its way. **It replaces the old restore suite** (owner, 15:43, asked: "Replace it"): the self-test's runner-based "restore" suite (eax/testing/restore_check.py) goes; `eax test restore` and `eax test backup-restore` both run the new test, and `eax test full` from the command line runs it last, after its confirmation. **The two drills are documents** (owner, 15:49, on the restore onto a new machine and the scheduled restore drill: "Thease are documents in the system to be read and made available for someone to manually do it. So document this for someone and also have them available as pdf for download. Steps in comman line"): two documents in Documentation (Run EA Explorer), each a numbered list of command-line steps a person follows by hand, with what to check after each, and a Download PDF on those two only (owner, 15:55: "we only needed pdf for drills not everything else"). **Further scenarios** (owner, 16 Nov 2026, 15:26: "If you think there should be more scenarios for backup and restore testing add them"), each in the same command-line run unless said, each checked, not assumed: **(S1) people and roles**: a user, a global role and a model role given between B1 and B2 are gone after B1 and back after B2 (Keycloak's database too), and the test accounts still sign in; **(S2) settings and persist/**: a setting, an alert level, a branding choice and a schedule changed between B1 and B2 follow the same way; **(S3) Release 5.5's data**: a discovery project with its sources, a run, Found in and skipped items, ADRs and dispensations with their links, the governance report; **(S4) what is worked out from the data comes back right**: search finds the restored elements, data quality, KPIs, the report views and the Superset dashboards (custom ones included) show the restored numbers, not those before the restore; **(S5) work goes on after a restore**: a new element, a commit, a tag, a document upload and a new ADR all succeed with no duplicate key (the numbering carries on: display IDs, ADR-<year>-<n>, commit numbers); **(S6) the jobs after a restore**: no job runs twice, none is left stuck, the scheduled ones start again on time; **(S7) a backup while people work**: B2 taken while the test keeps writing: the backup is one point in time across every database and the files (nothing half-saved, no document without its record or record without its file); **(S8) the same backup restored twice** gives the same checksums (a restore changes nothing of its own); **(S9) an application backup of one model and one project** (D233) restored into the same installation after they were changed, and into another model name, every row and file compared; **(S10) an older backup onto newer code**: a backup of the previous release restored, the migrations run forward, everything opens (forward only: a newer backup onto older code refused with the reason); **(S11) a restore cut off half-way** (the container stopped mid-restore): restored again it completes, and nothing half-restored is left serving; **(S12) not enough disk**: refused before anything is touched, saying how much is needed; **(S13) secrets**: the config file and the certificates come back; no password, key or token appears in the backup's logs or the test's report; **(S14) retention**: the scheduled backups' clean-up never deletes B1, B2 or a pinned backup; **(S15) the times and the loss said**: how long each backup and restore took and what would have been lost (the recovery time and point), in the report. The rest of this story stays as the way round it: Today the self-test's backup suite takes an infrastructure and an application backup and restores the test model from it as a new model, and the restore suite (the command line only) backs up, changes, restores and compares every part for the choices db, app+data, files and full and an application backup put back over itself (R2.45, the restore_check fingerprints). To build on it: (1) **the bug first**: "backup: an infrastructure backup" ended with RemoteDisconnected after about six minutes in the full test of 14 Nov (R4.24): the call that waits for a long backup is cut; the suite follows the backup as a background task instead and fails only on the backup's own result; (2) **every way a backup is taken and restored, tested**: from System Admin, the command line, the schedule, MCP (R4.22) and an update's own backup; a single model and a single project restored from its own backups (D233); a restore of each part on its own (each database, each file set, the config file, persist/); (3) **what comes back is checked, not only that it ran**: every database's rows and every file's checksum before and after (the fingerprints, extended to Keycloak's, Superset's, Mayan's, the library's and the views' databases and every file set), sign-in working afterwards (Keycloak), every report dashboard opening (R4.1), documents opening in Documents, the self-test's smoke run passing; (4) **a restore onto a new machine** (the disaster drill): a backup taken on one installation restored onto an empty one (another laptop or a server from `eax deploy`), with its config file, its certificate and persist/, and the installation working there; the time it took and the data lost said (the recovery time and point); (5) **broken backups refused**: a backup cut short, a changed file (its checksum), a backup of a newer version, a missing part and a full disk are each found by the backup's check (`eax backup verify`) and refused by a restore with the reason, nothing live touched; (6) **large data**: a backup and restore of a model and project load at R4.9's scale, timed, so its size and time are known before it is needed; (7) **a restore drill on a schedule** (monthly unless set): the newest backup restored into a scratch database and compared, the result on System Admin › Backups ("last proven restore") and an alert when it fails (R5.9); (8) a report of every backup and restore test with its times, sizes and what was compared (in the self-test's report and its Excel). Tests: the suite itself, run in the full self-test (restore remaining command-line only, as it takes the installation down) and by hand for the drill on a new machine. **To discuss with the owner before building, one question at a time**: whether the drill on a new machine runs in the self-test (a second installation) or by hand with a checklist; how often the scheduled drill runs; how long a restore may take before it fails. Not built until the owner says |

| R5.6-Q2 | **Built 16 Nov 2026, in 5.6.0 (D709)**. **Release 5.6: job priorities, low-priority scheduled jobs skipped when the queue is too full** (owner, 15 Nov 2026, 22:47: "Add another story for job priorities. If the queue is too full then low priorty reoccuring jobs may not be done like DQ check (automated), notifications etc"). Every kind of job gets a priority, shown and changeable on System Admin › Jobs: high (what people ask for: backups, restores, deliveries, merges; the alert checks), normal (the scheduled jobs that keep EA Explorer right: database checks, the change log, Keycloak upkeep...) and low (recurring jobs that can wait or miss a turn: the automated data quality checks, notification digests and reminders, KPI trends, the resource samples...). The job runner takes the highest first. **When the queue is too full** (more waiting than a set number, or the oldest waiting longer than a set time), a low-priority recurring job that comes due is not queued, or is taken off the queue if it is waiting, and its next run does the work instead; each one skipped is counted and shown (Jobs, and the alert jobs_stuck's message), so nothing is silently lost, and a low-priority job skipped too many times in a row is run anyway. **Agreed** (owner, 16 Nov 2026, 15:46): **low**: the data quality checks (quality_scheduler, quality_watch, quality_entities, quality_measure), the digests and reminders (quality_digest, surveys, servicedesk_overdue), the trends and samples (kpi_monthly, resource_sample, reports_refresh) and the background loads (industry_packs, sites_health, optimise_report, discovery_scheduler); **high**: what people ask for, backups, restores and the alert checks; every other kind normal. **Too full**: more than 20 waiting, or the oldest waiting over 10 minutes, both changeable on System Admin › Jobs. **A missed turn is not caught up**: the job's next run does the work; after 5 skips in a row it runs anyway; each skip counted on the Jobs page. Goes with R5.30 (the Jobs page) |
| R5.6-U1 | **Built 16 Nov 2026, in 5.6.0 (D710)**. **Release 5.6: Updates read GitHub Releases** (owner, 15 Nov 2026, 23:21: "the Updates should get data from the Releases from github and only from labels/branches if enabled"; placed in Release 5.6, 23:22). **The owner, 16 Nov 2026, 15:21: "Release should not be picked from the main branch, instead pick them from here - https://github.com/gauravvermaea/EA-Explorer/releases only upgrade forward"**: the versions offered are the published releases of that page only (gauravvermaea/EA-Explorer), never commits of `main`; only versions newer than the one installed are offered. **The repository is public** (owner, 15:47): read without a sign-in; a release's notes are its GitHub release text, or releases/<version>.md at its tag when that is empty. **Pre-releases** (owner, 15:35, asked: "Off unless switched on"): a release ticked pre-release on GitHub is the RC channel, offered only when a Super Admin or IT Admin switches RC on. Today System Admin › Updates finds versions in the repository's git tags (Prod: vX.Y.Z on `release`; RC: vX.Y.Z-rc.N on `rc`; Latest: commits of `main`) and reads the notes from releases/<version>.md at that tag (eaxcli/releases.py, D417, D498, D510). To build: (1) **Prod comes from the repository's published GitHub Releases**: each release's version, title, date and notes as published there; a draft is never offered; a tag with no published release is not offered on Prod; (2) **tags and branches only when switched on**: RC and Latest stay off until a Super Admin or IT Admin enables them on the Updates page, as today, and only then are tags and branches read; (3) the rules kept: forward only, one release at a time on the way, the backup and rollback, the notes shown before an update and in What's new; (4) when GitHub cannot be reached or refuses (its limit on calls without a sign-in), the page says so in words and offers nothing rather than guessing; what was last read is shown with its time; (5) the command line (`eax update`) and MCP read the same. Tests: published releases are offered with their notes, drafts and tags without a release are not; RC and Latest read nothing while off; GitHub down or over its limit is said, nothing offered. **To discuss with the owner before building, one question at a time**: whether a GitHub pre-release is the RC channel (instead of rc tags); whether releases/<version>.md stays as the fallback for notes; a private repository (a read-only token in the config file, never in seed data). Every password and key kept in a vault EA Explorer runs (OpenBao), not in the config file. Release 4 built the first part (R4.19, D684) and the owner switched it off the same evening ("For now disable openbao by default. No installation can use it"; "there should be no way or config for users to turn on openbao until I give details"): the code (eaxcli/vault.py, the openbao provider of eax secrets, the health and self-test checks, deploy/openbao/openbao.hcl) and its tests stay, switched off in the code; no container, volume, setting or command turns it on. **Nothing here is built until the owner has given the details and said so.** **Every earlier decision on it is to be taken again** (owner, 15 Nov 2026, 07:30: "When we do openbao all the decisions we had taken before need to be redone"): D684 and the answers given for R4.19 (it unseals by itself, Docker secrets, a server the same as a laptop, the keys in persist/), and D218 and D504 where they touch it, are starting points only, not decisions; R6.1 decides afresh, and the code built in Release 4 is changed or dropped to match |
| R6.1 | **Release 6: OpenBao, the design** (from R4.19). To discuss with the owner first, in detail: what it must protect against and from whom; laptop and server (whether each unseals by itself, D684, or a person unseals, D218, and where the unseal key lives); what it keeps (the 23 passwords and keys; discovery's sign-ins and the AI engine's keys, D671; webhooks' signing keys); how an installation from before moves in and back out; backups and restores of the vault and its keys; who may read a value and how each read is audited; what happens when the vault is down or its keys are lost; its memory. Then the parts built in Release 4 are checked against that design and changed where they differ. Not built until the owner says. **Also on the table** (owner, 15 Nov 2026, 07:35 to 07:36: "Why do we need openbao is it really needed?", "what happens if someone forgets key to unseal it?", "yes we can discuss it"): (a) **whether OpenBao is needed at all**: a key that unseals by itself sits on the same machine as the vault, so it protects little more than the config file locked to its owner; a person unsealing is safer but must type a key at every restart; the lighter way is Docker secrets (R6.2) with the outside vaults eax secrets already uses (a protected file, AWS Secrets Manager, Azure Key Vault, D504), and the switched-off OpenBao code deleted; (b) **a lost unseal key**: what is in the vault is gone for good (no reset, no back door); for EA Explorer that is the passwords, not the models, projects or documents, so an outage and a manual recovery (database logins set again in the database container, Keycloak's admin reset, Superset's secret key and saved connections made again, the Keycloak clients given new secrets); guarded against by splitting the key between people (for example any three of five), unsealing through a cloud key service or a hardware module with recovery keys, or a sealed copy kept offline, each with its own process (who holds what, leavers, a tested recovery); without OpenBao there is no unseal key to lose, as the passwords come back with every backup |

Needs each image tried on Docker. Not built until the owner says |

Candidates from what exists: (1) **one installation kept in step with another**, e.g. a development or test server with production, or a laptop with a server: models, metamodels, projects, documents and settings copied one way on a schedule or on demand (today: backups and restores by hand, application backups per model or project, D227); (2) **two or more live servers kept the same** (a standby or a second site), the database mirrored in real time: [story-real-time-mirrors.md](story-real-time-mirrors.md) (production deployment, phase 36); (3) **models shared between installations**, a model or a library pushed from one site to another and its changes merged back, through Sites and remote instances (R2.1, R2.2) and the merge wizard (phases 19, 20). | R6.5.1 | **Release 6.5: sync servers** (owner, 15 Nov 2026, 11:13: "This will basically take two instances and sync them - The idea is we can completly override, we can partially override we oveeride withwhat we want. The idea is things like migration etc"). Take two EA Explorer installations, a source and a target, and bring the target in line with the source, for migrations (a new server, a new site, a laptop to a server). Three ways, chosen each time: (1) **complete override**: the target becomes the source, everything replaced; (2) **partial override**: chosen parts replaced and the rest of the target kept; (3) **pick what to override**: the person chooses item by item (which models, metamodels, projects, documents, libraries, settings, people and roles). Before anything changes: a comparison of the two (what is only on one side, what differs, and versions), a preview of what will be added, replaced and removed, and a backup of the target. Run from System Admin and from `eax sync`, between Sites (R2.1, R2.2), each run audited and kept. To agree first: what is never carried over (passwords and keys, the target's own address and certificates, its change log), the versions the two must be on, how the two connect and sign in, and its tests. Not built until the owner says | planned |
| R6.5.2 | **Release 6.5: DR sync** (owner, 15 Nov 2026, 11:13: "Where in near realtime, batch content from primary is sent to DR"). A disaster-recovery installation kept close behind the primary: content sent from the primary to the DR site in small batches, near real time (every few minutes), one way only. The DR site is read-only while it is DR, shows how far behind it is (the last batch and its time), and is alerted on (R5.9) when it falls behind or a batch fails. A failover turns it into the primary on purpose, by a person, and a failback the other way. To agree first: what is sent (the databases, documents, the library, settings; the reports and Keycloak), how often, by what means (the database's own streaming or logical replication, or batches of changes from the change log, see [story-real-time-mirrors.md](story-real-time-mirrors.md)), how much can be lost (RPO) and how long a failover takes (RTO), how the two sites are secured, and a regular DR test. Not built until the owner says | planned |
| R6.5.3 | **Release 6.5: content promotion, dev to prod** (owner, 15 Nov 2026, 11:13: "new models, new content, new connectors, new incomming and outgoing feeds etc is first built on a dev server and then it can be promoted to a prod one. The URL's and credentials can be part of a file as we download and upload we can use zip uploads, git repo etc for this. It can be used to publish new libraries etc"). Content is built and checked on a development installation, then promoted to production: new models and metamodels, content, discovery connectors (R3), incoming and outgoing feeds (pushes, webhooks, OData), reports, project templates and libraries. (1) **A promotion package**: what was chosen, with its versions and a manifest, made on dev; (2) **the addresses and credentials kept apart**, in a file of their own per environment (dev values on dev, prod values given on prod), never in the package itself; (3) **carried as a zip** (downloaded from dev, uploaded to prod) **or through a git repository** (dev pushes, prod pulls a tag); (4) **on prod**: checked (its version, what it needs, signed if the installation uses R5.10's keys), a preview of what is added and changed, applied as a reviewed change, kept with its history, and rolled back if needed; (5) the same way **publishes a new library** or reference content to other installations. Run from the screens and from `eax promote`. To agree first: what can be promoted, what wins when prod has changed the same thing, who may promote, and its tests. Not built until the owner says | planned |
| R6.5.4 | **Release 6.5: a developer and author mode, and its menus** (owner, 15 Nov 2026, 11:17: "These things we just discussed need a develope and author mode and menus"). An installation (or a person) works in one of two modes, so R6.5.1 to R6.5.3 have a clear place: (1) **developer and author mode**, for a development or authoring server where new models, content, connectors, feeds, reports, templates and libraries are built and packaged for promotion; (2) **production mode**, where promoted content is received, previewed and applied, and changes made there are limited as the owner decides. The mode is shown on every page (as the environment badge is today), set by a Super Admin and kept in the config file, and decides what the menus offer: a menu of their own (for example **Environments**, or under System Admin) for **Sync servers**, **DR** (its state, how far behind, failover), **Promote** (make a package, send it, receive it, history) and the per-environment addresses and credentials file; `eax` the same. To agree first: whether the mode is per installation or per person (an author role), what production mode stops, the menu's name and place, and its tests. Not built until the owner says | planned |

Each guide written for the person doing it (a command or a screen at each step, what to expect, what to do if it differs), checked against what the product really does (the self-test where it can), available in Documentation and the Enterprise library like the other documents. Builds on docs/setting-up.md, deployment.md, operations.md and starting.md, R5.6-B1 (backup and restore tested) and Release 6.5 (syncing servers, DR sync). **Guides and guided tools** (owner, 21:14: "Documents and guided tools"): besides the documents, `eax` and the screens lead the way: `eax setup <laptop|server|cloud|dr|restore|rebuild|switch-to-dr>` and a Setup page in System Admin ask one thing at a time, run or show each step, check it worked and point to the guide's section. Which clouds are covered and the rest: to agree with the owner, one question at a time, when Release 6.6 comes up **Detail (owner, 16 Nov 2026, 07:37: "when we do documentation story we need to ensure more detailed documentation")**: every guide is step by step, for someone doing it the first time: what is needed before starting, each step with what to click or type and what the screen should show after it, how to tell it worked, what to do when a step fails (the messages one may see and what each means), and how to undo it; every screen the guides touch gets the same depth in its on-screen help |
| R6.6.1 | **Release 6.6: PostgreSQL integrity and the recent records, checked, and fixed when wrong** (owner, 15 Nov 2026, 21:31: "add a new story in 6.6 Postgres integrety and recent record check + fixing if issues are there"). For every database of the installation (EA Explorer's, Keycloak's, Superset's, Documents', the library's, the views'), on demand, after a restore, a rebuild from a backup or a switch to DR, and on a schedule: (1) **integrity**: the tables and indexes read whole and checked (PostgreSQL's own checks: amcheck on the indexes and tables, data checksums where on, every constraint and foreign key still holding, no orphan rows, sequences ahead of the rows they number); (2) **the recent records**: the newest records are there and as new as they should be (the latest change log entries, commits, tags, sign-ins, uploads and audit rows, compared with what the backup or the primary had), so a restore or a DR site is known not to have lost the last hours; (3) **fixing when there are issues**: what can be repaired is, with its preview and a backup first (an index rebuilt, a sequence moved on, a broken row's place reported), and what cannot is said plainly with the way out (the restore of that part from the newest good backup); every check and fix in the audit trail, shown on System Admin › Database maintenance (R5.12) and with `eax db`, an alert when a check fails. Builds on R5.12 (database maintenance) and R5.6-B1 (the restore fingerprints). The details (how often, what may be fixed without a person's yes): to agree with the owner, one question at a time, when Release 6.6 comes up |
| R6.6.2 | **Release 6.6: installing with an external database server the customer manages** (owner, 15 Nov 2026, 22:15: "in the release where we document all kinds of installations and DR etc, add installation with an external DB Server. So that we give an existing DB Server ourside of docker or something that customer manages"). A guide and the guided tool (`eax setup external-db`, and the Setup page) for an installation whose PostgreSQL is the customer's own server (on a machine of theirs, a cluster, or a managed cloud database), not the db container: (1) **what the customer's DBA prepares**: the PostgreSQL version and extensions needed, the databases and logins (the application's, the read-only reporting one, the installer's, Keycloak's, Reports', Documents', the library's and the views'), their rights, the connection limits, TLS, and the network path from the Docker host; a script they can review and run themselves; (2) **EA Explorer pointed at it**: the existing settings for where each database lives (R2.50, docs/databases.md: EAX_DB_HOST and the other databases' URLs) set by the tool, each tested before it is saved; the db container left out; (3) **what then works differently and how**: backups and restores (the customer's own database backups, or EA Explorer's dumps over the network, and which takes the lead), database maintenance (R5.12) and the integrity checks (R6.6.1) with the rights the customer gives, upgrades (migrations run by the installer login), monitoring and alerts; (4) **moving an installation** from the db container to the external server and back, and DR when the customer's server has its own replica. Builds on R2.50 (a database of its own) and D611. Details to agree with the owner, one question at a time, when Release 6.6 comes up |
| R6.6.3 | **Release 6.6: real certificates, guided and documented** (owner, 16 Nov 2026, 06:40: "In the whole setup story also discuss and add how real certificates will be used"; 06:50: all four ways chosen). Today the organisation's tls.crt and tls.key are put in EAX_TLS_DIR by hand (covering EAX_HOSTNAME, reports. and docs.), or a self-signed certificate is made, which browsers warn about. To build, each with its guide and a guided tool on screen: (1) **upload in the UI**: a Super Admin or IT Admin uploads the certificate, its chain and its key; it is checked before use (the names it covers, its dates, the key matching, the chain complete) and refused in words if wrong; the one in use is kept so a bad one can be undone; the key is never shown again or written to a log; (2) **an automatic public certificate** for a server reachable from the internet: EA Explorer gets one from a free public certificate authority (ACME) for the three names and renews it before it ends, with no hand work; (3) **the company's own certificate authority** for an internal server: its certificate with the chain, and a guide for trusting that authority on company laptops; (4) **expiry warnings**: alerts 30 days before a certificate ends and on the day, and a check in the full test. Every way: the certificate is backed up and restored with the rest (as today) and the DR guide says how it moves to the DR site; the self-signed one stays only for a laptop. **To discuss with the owner before building, one question at a time**: whether a new certificate takes effect without a restart; the DR site's own names; who may upload (Super Admin only, or IT Admin too). Not built until the owner says |

| R5.7-J1 | **Accepted 17 Nov 2026 in 5.7.0 (D739).** **Release 5.7: customer journey maps as first-class citizens** (owner, 16 Nov 2026, 07:52: "Not sure which release this should go in but add a story for customer journey maps will discuss in detail. Today a customer journey map is only one of the sample document templates on a project's "Other views and documents" tab (0.17.0): a file, not part of the model. To be first-class, a journey map would be modelled content like a capability map or a value stream: its own place on screen, its parts (the journey, its stages, steps and touchpoints, the people involved, how they feel, the pain points and moments that matter) held in the repository and linked to what already is (capabilities, value streams, business services, applications, data, actors and roles), drawn as a view, searchable and reported on, in the MCP for AI, governed, versioned and backed up like everything else. **Everything above is a starting point only, to discuss with the owner in detail, one question at a time, before anything is decided**: what a journey map holds, how it links to the metamodel, how it is drawn and edited, who may create and change one, and what reports it feeds. **Agreed so far** (owner, 16 Nov 2026, from 15:58, one question at a time): (a) **a journey map lives in a project, like the Business Model Canvas** (16:09, which replaces 15:59 "Model content"; 16:06: "keep it next to business model canvas in a tab"): free-standing from the models, **in tables of its own in the projects schema, as the canvas has** (16:12: "customer journey map also lives in its own tables in projects like business model canavs": proj.journeys, their items, documents and events, beside proj.canvases), on a **Customer journeys** tab next to the Business model canvas tab, its parts linking out to model elements (capabilities, value streams, business services, applications, data, actors and roles); (b) **its parts** (16:01): journey, stage and step; persona and touchpoint; emotion (a score at each step), pain point and moment that matters; opportunity (which can become a requirement or a work package); and **people may add their own kinds of part and fields** ("can we have them add things?"); (c) **shown both ways** (16:02): a **journey grid** to work in (stages as columns; rows for steps, touchpoints, the emotion curve, pain points and opportunities; a cell edited by clicking it; the linked model elements under each stage), and a **drawn view** made from it for presentations (draw.io); (d) **versions like the canvas** (16:11): kinds current state, target state, working copy and scenario; copies keep each item's lineage; two journeys compared (added, changed, removed); draft or agreed; every change kept; (e) **outputs** (16:12): exports like the canvas (PDF, PowerPoint and draw.io, of a journey or a comparison) and **MCP tools** to read and change journeys, so a person's own AI can draft one from interviews or documents (NOT FOR AI items titles only, as everywhere); not in the reports, and an opportunity is not turned into a requirement or work package automatically; (f) **who** (16:13): like the canvas: anyone on the project reads them, its contributors change them, and changing an agreed one makes it a draft again. Not built until the owner says **Also agreed** (owner, 17 Nov 2026): **the emotion at a step is scored from -2 to +2 with faces** (13:50: very unhappy, unhappy, neutral, happy, very happy), drawn as a curve above the steps with zero as the line; **the sample projects get a filled-in journey and there is a starter template** (13:51): a sample journey (current and target state, linked to its model, removed with the samples like the rest) and a blank template with typical stages people start from. **Built 17 Nov 2026 (D730; docs/customer-journeys.md).** |

The users can white lable it by 1. Adding Logo 2. Have skin 3. In this story add 3-4 themes to choose from. Today the screens have one look (styles.css, its colours in variables, a dark version when the computer asks for one) and the EA Explorer name and mark. To build: (1) **a logo**: an admin uploads the organisation's logo (and a small one for the browser tab), shown in the top bar of EA Explorer's screens, with the EA Explorer name kept small or replaced by the organisation's product name; (2) **a skin**: the look set once for everyone (colours, fonts, corners), the dark version included; (3) **3 to 4 themes to choose from**, shipped with EA Explorer (for example a light standard, a dark one, a high-contrast one for accessibility, a muted corporate one); the admin chooses the organisation's theme, and may let each person pick their own; (4) **CSS for the company**: an admin downloads the theme in use as a CSS file, changes it, uploads it back, and adds new ones, each with a name; an uploaded CSS is checked before use (only the look: colours, fonts, spacing; nothing that loads from elsewhere or runs) and a bad one never locks anyone out (the standard theme is always one click away, and on the break-glass sign-in); (5) the branding is backed up and restored with the rest and moved by a site copy. **Agreed so far** (owner, 16 Nov 2026, 16:14): **Super Admins** change the logo, the theme and the CSS; **each person may choose their own theme** from those offered when the admin allows it (a switch), the organisation's theme being the default; **four themes ship** (16:15): standard light (today's look), dark, high contrast (accessibility) and muted corporate. Agreed in full. **Also agreed** (owner, 17 Nov 2026, 13:49): the logo and theme are for **the screens only**; the files EA Explorer makes (PDF and Word exports, the canvas and journey exports, the reports' title pages) keep the EA Explorer mark. **Built 17 Nov 2026 (D729).** |
| R5.7-P1 | **Accepted 17 Nov 2026 in 5.7.0 (D739).** **Release 5.7: a project presentation deck, made with the AI through MCP** (owner, 16 Nov 2026, 09:57: "add a new feature project presentation deck. As with discovery (R5.5), EA Explorer is the source and the person's own AI does the work: in their MCP client the person talks the deck through with the AI, which reads what it needs from EA Explorer and builds the deck. To build: (1) **MCP tools that give the content**: a project's items (deliverables and artifacts, by phase and cycle), their documents and drawn views (as pictures), the models and views they refer to, the project's decisions, risks, requirements, key questions, sign-offs and status, each with where it came from, so a slide can say its source; only what the person may read (their own roles and the project's access); (2) **a tool that suggests an outline** from the project (for example the ADM phase it is in, what is done and what is open), for the AI and the person to change; (3) the deck itself is made by the AI in the person's client; documents marked "NOT FOR AI USE OR AUTOMATED PROCESSING" give their titles only. **Agreed so far** (owner, 16 Nov 2026): the finished deck **stays with the person** (16:16): EA Explorer only supplies the content; nothing is saved back; a deck may draw on **anything the person may read** (16:17): any project and model, by their own roles; the deck takes **the AI client's own look**, not the organisation's (16:17); **anyone signed in through MCP** may use the deck tools (16:18), getting only what their roles let them read. Seen on a Motivation view: Driver, Assessment, Goal, Outcome, Stakeholder and Grouping objects are plain coloured boxes with the type and code under the name, while the same view opened in draw.io shows each object with its ArchiMate icon in the corner (eax/notation.py, mxgraph.archimate3, D354, D396). To fix: every object on a view drawn on EA Explorer's own screens shows the **standard ArchiMate 4 icon of its type's notation**, the same one draw.io shows (an element with no ArchiMate glyph, or one of our extensions, stays a plain rounded box, as in draw.io); an entity's own icon, where one is set, keeps its place; colours, layout, the layer bands and the type and code line unchanged; the dark version and printing and the PDF and picture exports show the icons too. Built and run after every other 5.7 story, before 5.7 is accepted. (1) **the test's content grows with the release**: `eax test backup-restore` and `eax-drill` make, read back and check what 5.7 adds: a customer journey map (R5.7-J1: its stages, steps, personas, touchpoints, emotions, pain points, opportunities, a part of a kind added by people, a link to a model element, a target-state copy and a comparison), the branding (R5.7-B1: an uploaded logo, the organisation's theme, an uploaded CSS, a person's own theme) and, for R5.7-P1, that the deck tools still read a project after the restore; each gone after restoring B1 and back, exactly, after B2 and in the DR copy; (2) **the backup covers it**: the new tables and files are in every infrastructure backup, a project backup and a DR pack (the backup coverage test fails for a table or file set left out), and come back with a restore and a site copy; (3) **the owner runs** `eax test backup-restore` and `eax-drill` (and, if he wishes, a DR pack rebuilt with eax-dr), with the broad checks of D722: a run fails only when data is lost; (4) **anything they find is fixed** with a test that fails without the fix (D599), and run again until both pass. Not built until the owner says **Built 17 Nov 2026: the test makes, reads back and checks a customer journey, an uploaded theme and a person's own theme, and the deck tools after the restores (the organisation's logo and theme are left alone so the test never changes what everyone sees); for the owner to run with `.\eax test backup-restore` and `.\dr\eax-drill` before 5.7 is accepted.** |
| 40 | EA capability maturity (design agreed 9 Nov 2026, D377): how mature the organisation's architecture practice is, by **questions and evidence**: a questionnaire per area (governance, repository, process, skills, tools, value) sent as a survey (38.1) to chosen people, beside evidence EA Explorer measures itself (models in use, data quality, governance log use, projects following the ADM, sign-offs); a score per area out of 5, a target, gaps and a trend over assessments Built on the **US Department of Commerce Enterprise Architecture Capability Maturity Model (ACMM) version 1.2** (December 2007, a US government document with no usage restriction): six levels (0 None to 5 Measured), nine architecture elements, the characteristics of each element at each level as the assessment questions, and its two scoring methods (a weighted mean level, and the percentage achieved at each level per element), beside EA Explorer's own evidence. Taken from the DoC original, not from the TOGAF guide or screenshots of it (D379, D381). **Built 10 Nov 2026 (batch 1, D450 to D453): assessments as surveys with nine elements and six levels in our own wording, scores by both methods, targets and gaps, evidence kept, the trend**; version 0.17.0; **accepted and closed (owner, 10 Nov 2026)**; [phase-40.md](phase-40.md) |

The full plan is in [requirements/EA_Explorer_Build_Plan.xlsx](requirements/EA_Explorer_Build_Plan.xlsx); decisions in [decisions.md](decisions.md).
